#2244. Swansea City, Portsmouth & Barnsley in race to secure Aston Villa transfer agreement

Aston Villa academy graduate TJ Carroll is attracting loan interest from clubs in the EFL, with it expected that the youngster sources regular first-team football away from Villa Park in January.

That is according to the Daily Mail's Midlands football correspondent, Tom Collomosse, with the Championship pair of Portsmouth and Swansea City namedropped, as well as League One strugglers, Barnsley.

Villa are currently in the midst of another campaign involving European football under Unai Emery, with the Spaniard bringing home a first trophy in 30 years five months ago in the form of the UEFA Europa League.

The B6 club have given plenty of exposure to young talents throughout Emery's four-year tenure, and Carroll is one of many who have earned praise for their performance levels in the club's highly-successful youth setup.

The Villans have already sanctioned two loans involving Championship sides this campaign, with Joe Gauci and Samuel Iling-Junior joining Lincoln City and Bolton Wanderers respectively, and a third could be in store involving Carroll.

The centre-back has progressed through the Bodymoor Heath ranks from Under-9s level to now being on the fringes of Emery's new-look first-team.

Carroll is yet to make a senior appearance for Villa, but has featured against League One and League Two opposition so far this season in the form of Wigan Athletic and Crewe Alexandra in the EFL Trophy.

He signed professional terms with the West Midlands club in July 2025 having excelled in the Under-18s side which defeated Manchester City in the FA Youth Cup and National finals two months' previous, with his goal in the former initially levelling the scores before Villa emerged 3-1 victors.

It is now claimed that such progress has alerted Portsmouth, Swansea and Barnsley, with the South Coast side already agreeing three Premier League loans so far including Brentford's Benjamin Arthur, although the centre-back is currently sidelined with a stress fracture.

The Tykes have also aided the progress of the aforementioned Gauci in recent times, as the Australian goalkeeper was loaned to the South Yorkshire club in 2024/25, and they would hope for a repeat involving Carroll.

The Villans' initial second tier return in 2016 was one of immense chaos

Carroll's first-team exposure with Villa mainly came in pre-season ahead of the current campaign, which included outings against League Two promotion hopefuls Walsall, as well as FC Porto, Real Sociedad and BG Pathum United.

The youngster, who has 30 appearances at Under-21s level under his belt, has also featured in the UEFA Youth League, with Villa qualifying for the tournament through the exploits of Emery's first-team last term, and recently scored twice against FC Barcelona in a 3-3 draw.

Collomosse's report states that Pompey, the Swans and Tykes do face competition for Carroll's services in the form of Norwegian outfit, Viking FK.

The Stavanger-based outfit have gained notoriety in recent months for their own Champions League qualification, which includes a 3-3 draw with Bayern Munich, although their trip to Villa Park last month ended in a 3-1 loss.

With there being over a month until the January transfer window opens, circumstances may change which determine Carroll's potential destination, albeit one major call could have already influenced such decisions.

Swansea have conceded just 18 times in their first 15 Championship fixtures, but a poor run of recent results has now been compounded by Vitor Matos' controversial move to Ligue 1 side, RC Strasbourg.

#2243. Torbjorn Heggem to West Brom transfer truth emerges - 3 alternatives must be lined up

West Brom will hope to be active in the January transfer window as they look to beef up James Morrison’s squad.

Albion have enjoyed a very strong season so far, and they will feel that promotion is a genuine possibility, something which few thought possible before a ball was kicked back in August.

However, the Baggies merit their current position, and they have shown that they are capable of mixing it with the best in the Championship.

Yet, there will be concerns about their depth to last the distance, and fans will hope that owner Shilen Patel will provide the funds to bring in a few quality additions in the New Year.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

Therefore, recent suggestions that Albion should try and bring back Torbjørn Heggem were encouraging, as the Norway international is an excellent defender at this level, which is why Bologna paid West Brom £10m for the player back in 2025.

Given the finances involved in any deal, a return to The Hawthorns always seemed a long shot, and BBC reporter Steve Hermon has confirmed that Heggem won’t be joining Albion in January.

But, here we look at THREE alternatives Albion should be considering ahead of the winter window…

If Albion do want to bring a former player back to the club, then Cedric Kipre should be under consideration.

The powerful left-sided centre-back was very good during his time at the club, and he would bolster the backline, giving Morrison another strong, reliable figure in defence.

Kipre is currently at Ipswich, but after helping them to promotion last season, he is now way down the pecking order, to the extent that he often fails to make the matchday squad.

Therefore, he should be available in the January window, and he is the calibre of player that Albion should be looking to bring in, whether it’s on loan or permanently.

Another option for the Baggies is influential Stockport County defender Ethan Pye, who is an assured left-sided centre-back, who could also fill in at left-back if needed.

It wouldn’t be a straightforward deal to do, as the League One side are pushing for promotion, but the chance to join an Albion side that could be in the Premier League next season is going to appeal.

At 24, Pye is approaching his peak years, and he is capable of making the step up to the Championship, as he has all the attributes that you would want from a centre-back.

Plus, given his career so far, Pye would fit in with the rest of the Albion squad, who are a determined, hungry group, with most of them desperate to show they can play at a higher level.

Alternatively, Albion could try and repeat the Heggem trick, and one left-sided centre-back that could make the jump from a Scandinavian league to the Championship is Rosenborg’s Tomas Nemcik.

The 25-year-old has emerged as a key figure for the Norwegian side this season, and he has played an important role in helping them to third in the table.

At 6ft3in, he has the physical profile to cope with the demands of the Championship, and he is another player at a good age in terms of being able to make an instant impact, whilst also retaining value moving forward.

He won’t be a name that Albion fans recognise, but it’s hard to imagine many had heard of Heggem before he joined - and that worked out very well for the club!

#2242. Huddersfield Town & Notts County are cringing at the same thing right now

Tom Iorpenda was one of the EFL's brightest talents last season while at Notts County, but the 21-year-old has yet to see the pitch in any competition so far this year.

The former Brighton and Huddersfield Town academy product ended a five-year association with the Terriers this past summer, signing for Swansea City for around £1 million towards the end of the transfer window.

Iorpenda had played every game in league and play-off action for Notts in their League Two promotion campaign while on loan from Huddersfield last term, and returned to the Accu Stadium with plenty of momentum and a possible route into first-team action.

In the end, with his contract up at the end of the season, Huddersfield opted to cash in on the versatile midfielder and insert a sell-on clause so they could profit further down the line, instead of possibly losing him for free.

It's safe to say it hasn't worked out yet at all, which must be a real frustration for the League One pair.

It's always difficult when a young star arrives at a club in the latter stages of the summer transfer window. Iorpenda spent the whole summer and pre-season with Huddersfield, signing for the Swans on August 30.

Swansea adopted a much more intense play style ahead of Vitor Matos' second season in charge in South Wales. They rank first in pressing sequences, high turnovers and have the lowest PPDA (passes per defensive action) in the Championship.

That's difficult to get up to speed with when you have a full pre-season, let alone when you don't.

Additionally, Iorpenda joined a Swansea side that, at the time, had won two and drawn one of their first three games and were about to make it three wins from four against Watford on September 1.

So, the 21-year-old arrived at the club with just two Championship appearances under his belt, totalling 50 minutes, from his time at Huddersfield, has had to get up to speed with their intensity and has been trying to dislodge what has been a winning lineup.

It's been difficult, and that's the reason why he still has no appearances so far in a Swansea shirt.

Iorpenda was everywhere for Notts last year. He predominantly played as an attacking midfielder, but Martin Paterson trusted him to man the engine room a bit further back, too, and as loan spells go, it was one of the best in the EFL last year.

Not only did Iorpenda start 46 of the 49 league and play-off games at Meadow Lane last season, but he also scored three and assisted eight, including setting up Jayden Luker (another loanee set for bigger things) for the only goal over two legs in the play-off semi-final against Chesterfield.

Per FotMob, he ranked in the top 3% of players in his position for defensive contributions, duels won and tackles, but also in the top 5% for touches, passes and fouls won. He operated mainly in the left channel, but he covered ground better than most could in League Two that year.

Notts County would have likely had Iorpenda right at the top of their summer wishlist after they secured promotion from League Two last season, no matter how unrealistic the signing appeared to be. He was ready to propel himself up the leagues, and perhaps the Magpies could have been entrusted to continue his development in the tier above.

How disappointing has it been to see Tom Iorpenda hardly play this season, Notts fans? Let us know in the comments.

Unfortunately, the 21-year-old's contract situation worked against them, and now they're being forced to see him ride the bench in the Championship.

Iorpenda's stock has fallen quite significantly since he joined Swansea, which doesn't bode well for a Huddersfield side who felt they could have been earning a little more than the £1m they pocketed in the original sale.

It's unknown what contract talks were held between the midfielder and Huddersfield over the summer, but from his lack of inclusion in any side under Martin Drury to start the season, it was clear he wasn't favoured.

Even the Terriers couldn't deny that he has potential, though, and a £1m + sell-on clause actually went down as a good deal for both sides. Swansea got a high-potential player for cheap, and Huddersfield banked a seven-figure fee for a guy they could have lost for nothing at the end of the season.

At the moment, though, they aren't getting any more than that base amount, as Iorpenda is yet to showcase any Championship quality just yet, mainly because he's had no chance to showcase anything.

Notts will be looking on, annoyed that their ex-loan star isn't enjoying life elsewhere, while the dreams of making some more money immediately from Huddersfield's side aren't coming to fruition, either.

However, it's not as if Iorpenda has been left out of squads altogether. He's making the bench, just not getting on the pitch. In the 12 games he's been available for, he's made the 20-man squad in half of those.

He'll be one of the players looking to impress whoever the newhead coach turns out to be, after Vitor Matos left Swansea on Wednesday to take charge at Ligue 1 outfit Strasbourg.

A look at the 25 biggest clubs in England according to AI chatbot ChatGPT.

A potential new playstyle could be implemented that benefits Iorpenda, or the new boss could simply come into the job with a fresh slate, and the ex-Huddersfield man won't already be battling with settled starters.

Either way, it may only be a matter of time before Iorpenda gets to see the pitch in a Swansea shirt for the first time, and those at Huddersfield and Notts County who have cringed at his involvement so far will hope he can make a name for himself when he does.

#2241. Two days or two weeks – Michael Carrick future decision rolls into the international break

Manchester United will use the November international break to decide whether Michael Carrick stays on as head coach, after a three-game reprieve that delivered only one win.

According to The Guardian, Carrick came close to being sacked after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October.

Director of football Jason Wilcox and chief executive Omar Berrada discussed his position at the training ground the following day and agreed to give him three matches to turn things around before the break.

Those games brought a 2-0 defeat at Chelsea, a 3-2 win over Roma and a chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday. United conceded eight goals across the three fixtures, and defensive frailty remains the biggest worry.

Wilcox is now leading a review of Carrick’s position. He will consult Berrada before making a recommendation to Sir Jim Ratcliffe, who oversees the club’s sporting operations. Talks are expected to begin on Monday and continue through the break.

The league table makes grim reading. United sit in the Premier League relegation zone with just one league win in their last 10 matches.

If he does win a stay of execution, results will need to follow. Manchester United cannot be languishing in 17th place.

Carrick remains defiant. Speaking to Sky Sports News after the Villa draw, he admitted his side “haven’t won enough games” but insisted he expects to move forward.

“I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it’s got to win games, said Carrick after the late Villa draw.

“I think there’s more than enough [to turn things around].”

If United do make a change, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe is reported to be Wilcox’s preferred candidate. Howe has been out of work since leaving St James’ Park during pre-season, after four and a half years in charge.

In the meantime, there are no plans to strengthen Carrick’s coaching staff.

For now, Carrick is expected to be in charge for United’s next match, against Liverpool. Whether three games were enough to convince the hierarchy should become clear before then.

The trip to Anfield could be do or die for the decorated former United midfielder.

He’s not likely to survive a heavy defeat at the home of the club’s fierce rivals, but a galvanising win would surely buy him time and goodwill.

That’s if he makes it through the next fortnight before then. A decision or indication could be two days or two weeks away.

The only certainty at Old Trafford is uncertainty.

The latest chapter is another muddled process.Wilcox and Berrada met at Carrington the day after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October and agreed to give Carrick three more matches.A defeat at Chelsea, a win over Roma and a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa later, they appear to have reached the conclusion many expected a fortnight ago.

#2237. “Quite simply disgusting”: Gary Neville tears into Manchester United over Carrick sacking

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Gary Neville has launched a furious attack on Manchester United’s leadership over the way the club handled Michael Carrick’s dismissal.

United confirmed on Thursday that Carrick had been sacked, four days after the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa left them 18th in the Premier League with eight points from 10 games.

The club said it had reached the “extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary”.

Neville had already branded the process “classless” on The Overlap’s Stick to Football podcast before the announcement, pointing out that it was widely known Carrick was about to go while United were holding talks with potential replacements.

Speaking to Sky Sports after the decision was confirmed, the former United captain went even further.

“It’s just quite simply disgusting,” he said.

“That falls well below the standard of what you would expect in any other business, in any other industry. It would be constructive dismissal and you’d be getting sued.”

Neville took particular aim at the decision to hold the crucial meeting in Monaco, with chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox flying out to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe while Carrick was still taking training at Carrington.

“The optics of being in Monaco whilst the team, the staff, the people who are running the club on a day-to-day basis are in Manchester, it’s just not good and it reflects badly on the leadership,” he said.

It seems the only ones not to realise that are the trio above, but they aren’t stupid. How they explain it, or attempt to rationalise, it will be painful to listen to.

Neville also argued the club should have acted sooner. Looking back to the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth in October, he said United “effectively watched Michael Carrick resign on the pitch” as he waved goodbye to supporters at full time, only for the board to hand him three more games.

He summed up the episode as “indecision, lack of authority, just basically really poor leadership”.

It’s more than an episode, it’s an omnishambles that reflects the dysfunctional state of MUFC in its current guise.

United are now expected to move quickly for Mauricio Pochettino. The United States head coach is reported to have reached an agreement in principle with the club, which is prepared to pay his release clause.

First, they will need to wait until the USMNT completes its CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final double header against Haiti on Saturday and Wednesday.

Yes. Man United to wait until an international break to appoint an international manager…

For Neville, though, the bigger problem lies above the dugout and no manager can be the scapegoat for that massive issue.

#2236. BREAKING: Mauricio Pochettino agrees Manchester United deal to become new manager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have confirmed an agreement with Mauricio Pochettino to become their next head coach, with the Argentine set to take charge after the international break.

The 54-year-old will replace Michael Carrick, who was dismissed on Thursday following United’s disastrous start to the Premier League season. Pochettino is set to sign a two-and-a-half-year contract that will keep him at Old Trafford until the summer of 2029.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea boss is currently managing the United States national team but is expected to fly straight to Manchester after completing his USA international games.

Pochettino will first oversee the USA’s CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final ties against Haiti on Saturday and next Tuesday. He is expected to arrive in Manchester on Wednesday before taking charge of the club on Thursday.

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Carrick’s departure came less than six months after he signed a two-year contract. His second spell in charge ended after United managed just one win in their opening 10 league matches, leaving them in the relegation zone.

Carrick’s coaching staff, including Jonny Evans, Jonathan Woodgate, Steve Holland, Travis Binnion and goalkeeping coach Craig Mawson, have all left alongside Carrick.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are currently in the Premier League relegation zone after winning just one of their opening 10 league matches.

Their defensive record has been shambolic, and Pochettino needs to fix it as soon as possible. They have conceded 19 goals in the Premier League this season, the third fewest after Crystal Palace and Ipswich Town.

Their defensive struggles could encourage the Old Trafford board to invest in a new centre-back, with Newcastle’s Malick Thiaw emerging as a potential option.

Amid their defensive struggles, Manchester United are among several clubs keeping tabs on Malick Thiaw as they look to address their problems under new manager, Mauricio Pochettino.

Newcastle United have set a massive € 80 million asking price for Malick Thiaw amid growing interest in the German international.

Several top European clubs have the 25-year-old centre-back on their radar after his impressive performances at St James’ Park. However, Newcastle are determined to not lose him on a cheap.

According to reports by Sky Germany, Matthias Jaissle’s side would want at least €80m for Thiaw, who remains under contract with the Magpies until 2029.

The German defender has also earned recognition from national team manager Jurgen Klopp, while Jaissle considers him an important part of his Newcastle side.

Newcastle signed the former Schalke defender from AC Milan in 2025 for a reported €35million.

Since arriving at St James’ Park, Thiaw has established himself as a key figure in the Magpies’ defence, making 64 appearances and contributing five goals and two assists.

His performances have attracted attention from several clubs, but Newcastle are in a strong position given his long-term contract.

Although a departure next summer has not been entirely ruled out, the Magpies are unlikely to entertain any approach that falls short of their €80million valuation.

Manchester United have a clear gap to fill in their defence line. Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez haven’t eased the nerves this season and the Red Devils need a change at the back. Malick Thiaw can be the answer but he isn’t going to be cheap cost United heavy on their pockets.

The big question isn’t the transfer value but whether he is the profile that Mauricio Pochettino needs when he rebuilds Manchester United.

#2233. Pochettino begins Man Utd reign ahead of Liverpool showdown

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Mauricio Pochettino officially begins work as United head coach on Wednesday, just four days before his first match against Liverpool at Anfield.

The 54-year-old replaces Michael Carrick, who was dismissed last week after United won just one of their opening 10 Premier League matches, leaving them 18th in the table.

Pochettino arrives after completing his final assignment with the United States, who beat Haiti 2-0 on Tuesday to secure an 8-0 aggregate victory in their CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final.

The Argentine has signed a contract until the summer of 2029 and is expected to meet his new players and oversee his first training session on Thursday.

Speaking after his final match with the United States, Pochettino acknowledged the challenge awaiting him in Manchester.

“The new challenge is a big challenge, but at the same time it’s adrenaline you want to feel,” he said.

Several members of his coaching staff, including long-time assistant Jesus Perez, travelled to Manchester earlier this week to begin preparations. Pochettino will have limited time to work with his squad before Sunday’s Premier League meeting with Liverpool, which kicks off at 4.30pm GMT.

The former Tottenham, Chelsea and Paris Saint-Germain manager inherits a United side struggling for results after a difficult opening to the campaign. He previously guided Tottenham to the 2019 Champions League final and won the French league title during his spell with Paris Saint-Germain.

United’s next fixtures also include a Champions League trip to Sporting CP and a home Premier League match against Brentford. Pochettino’s immediate task is to improve United’s league position, beginning with a demanding first match at Anfield.

Now the next task is to replicate that on the road, where they've failed to win since their first away game of the season against West Ham United, but it won't be easy against a Wolves side who are within touching distance of top spot.

There are still parts of the Old Gold fanbase who are sceptical of head coach Cesar Peixoto, but one thing they can agree on is that, Bolton Wanderers aside, they've been well in control of their home games this season.

Both sides will be keen to take all three points, but these eight players won't be available to help do so.

#2231. ‘Sometimes to love is to let go’, former Man Utd midfielder announces retirement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Former Manchester United midfielder Juan Mata has announced his retirement from professional football at the age of 38, bringing an end to a beautiful career of around 20 years.

The Spaniard went on to win one of the biggest trophies in professional football. The former Manchester United midfielder confirmed his decision on Wednesday after ending his playing career with Australian side Melbourne Victory.

Mata enjoyed a memorable spell at Manchester United after arriving from Chelsea in January 2014. He spent more than eight years at Old Trafford, making 285 appearances, scoring 51 goals and winning four trophies.

His first trophy with Manchester United came in the 2016 FA Cup final against Crystal Palace. He also helped the club win the Community Shield, League Cup and Europa League during the 2016/17 campaign.

Before moving to Manchester, Juan Mata had established himself as one of Chelsea’s most creative players. He won the club’s Player of the Year award in both of his first two full seasons at Stamford Bridge before eventually joining United as David Moyes’ marquee signing.

Mata probably had the biggest moments of his career in the Spain shirt. He earned 41 international caps and scored 10 goals, becoming part of the most talented Spanish team in history. He went on to win the 2010 FIFA World Cup and Euro 2012 with Spain while etching his name in Spanish football history with a goal in Spain’s 4-0 victory over Italy in the European Championship final.

After leaving Manchester United in 2022, the midfielder continued his career with Galatasaray, Vissel Kobe and Western Sydney Wanderers before joining Melbourne Victory in September 2025.

His final season in Australia was an impressive one. Mata registered five goals and 13 assists, won the Johnny Warren Medal and earned a place in the A-League Team of the Season.

Reflecting on his decision, Mata spoke about the reason that kept him playing for so many years.

“I was able to keep going for so many years because of the sheer joy of playing,” he said.

He also made it clear that retirement would not mean walking away from football altogether.

“Football will never leave my life. There are so many ways to continue loving the game.”

Manchester United also paid tribute to their former midfielder, writing on social media: “Great player, better person. Thank you for representing our colours with class, Juan.”

#2230. Manchester United eye sensational former Liverpool signing as Glazer family consider major shake-up

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United could make an ambitious move for former Liverpool sporting director Michael Edwards, with the Glazer family reportedly keen to bring the highly rated executive to Old Trafford.

According to a report from popular Indykaila News on X, the Glazer side of United’s ownership wants Michael Edwards to take full control of the club’s football operations.

Exclusive: Manchester United board member eyes extraordinary move for Michael EdwardsA Manchester United board member is pushing for the appointment of former Liverpool director of football Michael Edwards. A major U-turn from Edwards would be needed to join the #MUFC project.…

With the Glazers interested in the appointment, such a move would be a major shift from the current arrangement at Old Trafford, where Sir Jim Ratcliffe and INEOS are currently responsible for overseeing the football department.

Edwards is regarded as one of the most influential executives in modern English football, having played a major role in building Liverpool’s squad under Jurgen Klopp.

Michael Edwards joined Liverpool in 2011 before being appointed as their sporting director in November 2016. He oversaw player recruitment bring in some of the biggest names at Liverpool including Mo Salah and Sadio Mane. Liverpool saw one of their most successful years in modern history in the Premier League and Europe under Jurgen Klopp while Edwards oversaw the footballing operations.

Manchester United have previously attempted to bring Edwards to Old Trafford before, but were unsuccessful.

Following his departure from Liverpool in 2022, Edwards turned down approaches from several clubs, including Manchester United and Chelsea. He then returned to Fenway Sports Group in March 2024 to oversee FSG’s wider football operations.

His second spell with FSG came to an end in July 2026. That exit has reopened the door for many interested clubs including Manchester United. However, there is no confirmation that Edwards is considering another move to United.

#2229. Who are Mauricio Pochettino’s assistant coaches at Manchester United?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United parted ways with Michael Carrick after a disastrous start to the Premier League season with just one win from 10 games.

Manchester United have spent a reported 12 million to bring Mauricio Pochettino and his coaching team from the United States to Old Trafford, with the Argentine determined to work alongside several familiar faces.

United paid around $10 million to secure Pochettino’s release from the US team and with a further $2 million reportedly required to secure members of his backroom staff.

The 54-year-old has built a close working relationship with his coaching team throughout spells at Espanyol, Southampton, Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain and Chelsea. Several of those staff members have followed him to Manchester United.

Jesus Perez is Pochettino’s long-time assistant and is widely regarded as his right-hand man. Their relationship began at Espanyol, where Perez initially worked as an analyst before becoming his assistant at Southampton. He has followed the Argentine throughout his career with spells at Tottenham, PSG and USA. He is now set to take the same role at Old Trafford.

Jimenez will work as United’s goalkeeping coach. He first joined Pochettino’s coaching setup at Espanyol in 2011 and has remained alongside him throughout his managerial career, including their time in England, France and the United States.

The manager’s 31-year-old son has also joined the coaching group. Sebastiano started working with his father during their time at Tottenham and has continued as a sports scientist and fitness coach with him with the USA team. He is also joining Pochettino at Manchester United.

The former Argentine defender has worked with Pochettino since his first managerial job at Espanyol. He has been involved in training sessions, video analysis and tactical preparation, helping the manager develop game plans.

The arrivals mean a major change behind the scenes at Carrington following the departures of Michael Carrick and his coaching staff.

Mauricio Pochettino is set to face Liverpool in his first game as Manchester United head coach on Sunday.

#2228. Is Mauricio Pochettino’s job at Manchester United the toughest in the Premier League?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United hired Mauricio Pochettino as their next manager to replace Michael Carrick, who managed just one win in 10 games.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

As he faces Liverpool for his first game on Sunday, the question arises: is he facing the toughest job in the Premier League?

The question was posed on BBC Radio about who is going to have more pressure, Pochettino or Thomas Frank at Crystal Palace.

According to former Premier League defender Nedum Onuoha, the expectations surrounding Manchester United will make the Argentine’s job considerably harder.

Pochettino is preparing for his first game in charge of Manchester United, with Liverpool waiting at Anfield on November 22. However, the challenge facing the new manager goes beyond getting results on the pitch.

Onuoha believes Pochettino will have to deal with constant discussion and criticism about his work, much of it coming from former United players who are now in the media.

Choosing between Pochettino and Frank, Onuoha said, “Probably Pochettino, because every former Manchester United player works in the media, so there will always be discussion around what he is doing.

“Whereas Frank can just enjoy playing his football at Crystal Palace. If he does well then it is great, if he doesn’t do well then it is still OK.

“But from Pochettino’s perspective, if Manchester United finish the season 15th in the Premier League, whether we like it or not, there will be some criticism of him because he’s had 28 games to do something.”

The comments just highlight the difference in expectations at the two clubs. Frank may have more freedom to work without the same level of attention, while Pochettino will be judged from day one against the standards historically associated with Manchester United. That is one big problem for Pochettino.

Rory Smith from The Observer highlighted another glaring problem at United.  Highlighting a major problem, Smith said, “there is a cycle element to Manchester United whereby a manager comes in, makes things a bit better, and then within a year to 18 months they aren’t challenging for titles again because the Premier League has changed since they were a dominant force.”

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22. That opening fixture will give him an immediate test, but the bigger task will be to get back United to winning ways, step by step.

Will the Manchester United job be without pressure for Mauricio Pochettino? Absolutely Not.

#2227. Manchester United consider legal action over JJ Gabriel departure

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Manchester United are prepared to consider legal action if evidence emerges that another club approached academy forward JJ Gabriel without permission.

The 16-year-old announced his departure from Old Trafford in a social media video on Tuesday and is expected to join Real Madrid in January. According to The Times and ESPN, United remain concerned about the circumstances surrounding his proposed move, although no breach of transfer regulations has been established.

Gabriel has been absent from United since September, when he requested the cancellation of his registration. The teenager remains registered with the club, and United could explore legal or regulatory options if evidence of an unauthorised approach emerges.

The Independent reports that United do not want to punish Gabriel for actions they believe may have been taken by adults involved in his future. Real Madrid have been widely linked with the forward, but his transfer has not been formally completed.

Gabriel joined United’s academy in 2020 and was named the club’s young player of the year last season. He featured for the senior side during pre-season but has not made a competitive first-team appearance.

The youngster is eligible for a move within the European Union under an exception to FIFA’s rules on international transfers involving players aged under 18. In his farewell message, Gabriel thanked United for helping him develop during his six years at the club.

His expected move to Madrid would bring that association to an end, although questions remain over the process leading to his departure. United have not announced legal proceedings, and there has been no finding of wrongdoing by Real Madrid or any other club.

#2226. EFL club officially confirm they're going to reduce capacity of their stadium

Mansfield Town have made a decision to decrease the capacity of the One Call Stadium, otherwise known as Field Mill, after a meeting with the Safety Advisory Group.

The Stags are currently in the midst of a third successive campaign in League One, with Nigel Clough's side amassing 19 points from 12 matches.

As a result of the club's recent trajectory, Mansfield have welcomed several fallen giants to this part of Nottinghamshire.

Stags fans have developed a reputation for generating plenty of noise, which, in turn, has led to a strong atmosphere with those backing the visiting sides housed in the stadium's North Stand.

However, the decision, which has been made ahead of Saturday's clash with MK Dons, hasn't gone down well with several fans.

The One Call Stadium is currently the fifth-smallest stadium in League One, with only the home grounds of Cambridge United, Stevenage, Burton Albion and Bromley able to house fewer spectators.

Attendances so far this season have been at the 8,527 mark on average, but Mansfield will now see fewer visiting supporters pass through the turnstiles.

A statement from Nottinghamshire Council on Friday confirmed that the stadium's North Stand will be reduced in capacity from 1,000 to 600 following incidents relating to crowd management and control in the EFL Trophy clash against Chesterfield, which the League Two side won 5-3.

Mansfield have since confirmed the agreement in conjunction with the council and Safety Advisory Group, but also confirmed that the club are exploring the possibility of a safe-standing area within the stand.

Although this decision doesn't have a major impact on the matchday experience of the home support at the One Call Stadium, other than the fact there are fewer visiting fans to engage in back-and-forth with, it has caused plenty of responses.

One Stags supporter wrote: "SAG vs Mansfield Town yet again. Do they ever work with the club rather than against it?"

"But why? What was said in the meeting? Away fans getting punished by the SAG which reflects on the club? They deserve a bit of clarity," another commented.

"Poor decision and tbh, it’s rarely full," backed up the feeling of confusion as to why such measures have been taken.

Another Mansfield fan debated: "Surely (has) to be done up and down the country then?

"There is literally nothing wrong with away fans standing up. SAG need to give their head a wobble," was one of many strong reactions.

With Mansfield's last two home games coming against the Spireites and Notts County, another questioned: "Did SAG not look at who the last 3 games have been against at home? At least 2 they should’ve known it would’ve been an issue."

One of the potential fans visiting from Buckinghamshire on Saturday said: "What('s) wrong with standing up? No need to close off part of the stand because of it."

What do you make of Mansfield Town's decision to reduce the One Call Stadium's capacity? Comment below!

However, a Port Vale fan agreed with the decision, replying: "The ticket regulations say that fans should be sitting down. If they stand up and refuse to sit then have them thrown out. If they refuse to leave then have police arrest them. Don't punish other fans because there are some idiots."

Although Mansfield clarified that the allocation will be temporarily reduced, it has already been debated for how long this will be the case.

Recent fixtures have clearly impacted this decision, and some of League One's largest fanbases could be left scrambling for tickets depending on how long such measures are in place.

Indeed, the Stags are still yet to host the likes of Barnsley, Sheffield Wednesday and Leicester City, with it not exactly being an arduous journey for those who follow the Tykes, Owls and Foxes to make.

FLW have ranked the 10 best EFL League One stadiums so far in the 2026/27 season, including Bradford City and Sheffield Wednesday.

Ironically, the decision to house fewer away supporters could still play to the advantage of Clough's side, who have picked up the majority of their points so far on home turf.

#2225. Manchester United linked with Nico Paz as Real Madrid retain buy-back option

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Manchester United have been linked with Como midfielder Nico Paz, although Real Madrid’s buy-back option and the absence of reported negotiations leave considerable uncertainty over a potential move.

The 22-year-old Argentina international has impressed in Italy this season, scoring six goals and providing eight assists in 14 appearances. According to The Sun, United have been monitoring Paz ahead of a possible summer transfer, with Mauricio Pochettino’s appointment potentially helping their chances.

Paz joined Como from Real Madrid in 2024 and has become an important player under Cesc Fabregas. He scored twice and provided an assist in Como’s 4-1 Champions League victory over United last month.

However, Madrid confirmed in June that they retain an option to re-sign the midfielder for the 2027-28 season. The clause is reportedly worth €80m (£67.6m), although the Spanish club have not publicly confirmed that figure.

Transfer journalist Fabrizio Romano has questioned the strength of United’s reported interest, saying there has been no contact or serious negotiation. Romano explained that the link had emerged because the same agency represents Paz and Pochettino.

He also suggested that Madrid remain central to the player’s future and that a January move is not being considered. United could assess their attacking midfield options next summer, with captain Bruno Fernandes’ contract situation still unresolved.

For now, Paz remains a reported target rather than a player involved in active transfer talks.

#2222. Who stays and who leaves Manchester United under Mauricio Pochettino, Gary Neville reveals list

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United hired Mauricio Pochettino as their next manager to replace Michael Carrick, who managed just one win in 10 games.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Pochettino takes charge of Manchester United sitting in an unfamiliar 18th in the Premier League table with only one win from 10 matches. He has a massive job ahead of him, with many pundits and former players terming his situation the toughest in the Premier League.

Gary Neville believes the Argentine will need several transfer windows to get the squad where he wants it, if he gets that much of time at Manchester United.

The former captain shared his views on which players he expects Pochettino to keep, and who could be heading for the exit.

Neville believes Senne Lammens should remain part of the plans, although he expects Pochettino to bring in another goalkeeper to give him some competition for his place. At the back, he backed Luke Shaw, Harry Maguire and Leny Yoro to stay, while suggesting Ayden Heaven needs a loan move to the Championship.

“Heaven, I’d loan him out, get some experience in the Championship. He’s nowhere near it, it’s unfair to put him in the situation he’s in,” Neville said on Stick to Football.

In midfield, Neville wants Pochettino to build around Bruno Fernandes, Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans. He also believes Andrey Santos could have a future at the club because of his age, mobility and aggression.

Mason Mount, however, is not part of his plans. Neville said: “Mount would have to go” and identified him as the player to leave from that midfield group.

Neville also has his doubts on the forward line, with much of the front line seeing a change. Gary Neville expects Joshua Zirkzee to leave and believes United cannot continue paying Marcus Rashford’s wages.

“Zirkzee definitely to go. I think Marcus 100 per cent to go because he’s on massive money and that story has been well told. They can’t afford to pay him that money, you have to get rid of that wage at the earliest opportunity.”

He expects Pochettino to work with Amad Diallo, Bryan Mbeumo, Matheus Cunha and Benjamin Sesko. Patrick Dorgu could also be useful in the short term, although Neville thinks he may leave within the next couple of years. Manuel Ugarte, meanwhile, was another player he was certain would go.

Talking about the exits, Gary Neville has singled out many United stars to be shown the door in the first wave of squad clearance by Mauricio Pochettino. Neville identified Lisandro Martínez, Matthijs de Ligt and Noussair Mazraoui as further candidates to leave in the first wave of departures, alongside Mount, Rashford and Zirkzee.

Neville believes another group of exits could follow a year later, with Shaw, Maguire, Diogo Dalot and Fernandes among the players whose futures may not be at the club in long term. Pochettino would also have to decide whether Santos and Carlos Baleba fit into his long-term plans.

Neville does not expect United to rebuild the squad in a single summer, even if Pochettino makes a positive start.

“But the earliest Mauricio Pochettino can unravel this squad is two seasons if he does well. It could take three to get this squad unravelled.”

Pochettino’s first game comes against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday. His immediate priority will be to get some results for Manchester United but beyond the immediate games and results, Manchester United are expected to make multiple exits from the club as Pochettino settles into the job.

#2220. How can Mauricio Pochettino help Manchester United players to perform again?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino has called on Manchester United to rediscover their winning mentality after agreeing to become the club’s new head coach.

Manchester United announced Pochettino’s appointment just over 24 hours after confirming the departure of Michael Carrick. The former Chelsea and Tottenham manager is believed to have agreed a two-and-a-half-year deal at Old Trafford.

He takes charge of United who sit in an embarrassing 18th place in the Premier League table. They have managed just one win in their opening 10 league matches, coming against Ipswich Town.

There is little time for the new head coach to settle in, with a difficult first fixture against fierce rivals, Liverpool already awaiting him.

Speaking to Manchester United, Mauricio Pochettino made it clear that restoring confidence in the team and improving performances will be his immediate priorities.

“The most important thing for now is to start to work hard to recover that feeling about being a winner – to win games, to perform.”

The Argentine hinted at sticking with the current crop of players to help them rediscover their form.

“We need to love the players that we have. We need to support the players, we need to provide all the tools for them to perform.”

Pochettino’s first match in charge will be one of the toughest possible introductions to English football’s biggest rivalry. Manchester United travel to Anfield to face Liverpool on Sunday, giving Pochettino very little time to work with his squad before his first outing.

The new head coach faces a mountain ahead of him. He has walked into one of the most difficult jobs in world football. United have struggled so far into the season and they need results quickly to get them out of the relegation spots.

Pochettino has spoken about bringing back the winning feeling at Old Trafford, but his first challenge will be getting a response from his players who have managed only one league win in 10 games so far. That is far from Manchester United standards and Pochettino will be hoping he can instill some confidence back in the team.

The Liverpool game will offer an early indication of how his players respond to the change at United. A positive result would provide immediate relief for the under pressure United but the longer-term job of rebuilding United will take much more than one match.

#2219. Barnsley make Huddersfield Town ticket announcement and it's caused backlash

Barnsley have confirmed that adult tickets for their game against Huddersfield Town next month will cost £35.

The Tykes have had a tough season so far, with Daniel Stendel’s return to Oakwell not producing the results that many had hoped, as the team sit in the relegation zone.

However, many fans recognise that Barnsley’s problems run deeper right now, and there are calls for the owners to leave the club.

Whilst the criticism towards those at the top of the club is wide-ranging, one aspect that has left fans annoyed is the price it costs to watch games.

And, that has ignited again, with the club confirming that adult tickets for the home clash against Huddersfield on December 19 will be £35.

This was always going to be a game that was priced higher than most, as there is a rivalry between the two Yorkshire clubs - and it’s worth noting it’s the same pricing structure as the one they went with against Sheffield Wednesday.

Plus, with Huddersfield flying high, they are likely to shift a lot of tickets, so Barnsley, who will be charging the visitors £35 as well, will see this as a chance to make money.

Firstly, it should be said that problems with ticket prices are not exclusive to Barnsley, and fans of clubs up and down the country would want their own clubs to review prices.

But, in this instance, you can understand why Barnsley fans are furious, as £35 is an awful lot of money to watch a League One fixture.

Barnsley will be wincing at the latest news out of League One involving Bradford and a potential takeover development mirroring Sheffield Wednesday's.

Yes, it’s a game against a local side, but Barnsley are battling to stay in the third tier right now, so they should be doing all they can to bring supporters back, which isn’t going to happen when you’re charging £35.

Plus, this is a game that will be played a week before Christmas, at a time when money is tight for everyone. When you combine that with the fixture being televised, it really is a poor decision from Barnsley, who are prioritising money over fans.

Again, they’re by no means the only club to do that, but it’s a shame that they aren’t going the other way and trying to do affordable deals to entice supporters back.

Of course, the hierarchy will argue that they need to bring in funds to help the club, but it’s always about striking the right balance and making fans feel valued.

What do you make of Barnsley's ticket prices? Let us know in the comments below.

As you would expect, this development didn’t go down well with the Barnsley fans, with many making it clear that they feel this is far too much money to ask fans to pay.

However, there was one Barnsley fan who did understand the thinking of the Barnsley hierarchy, as he explained why it made 'commercial sense'.

Meanwhile, these Huddersfield fans couldn't resist having a dig at their rivals, as they joked that £35 was more than fair to get to watch Martin Drury's team, and one player in particular.

#2218. What did the secret drone footage reveal about Manchester United training?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino’s first Manchester United training session was secretly filmed by a drone operator, who has since apologised and removed the footage from his YouTube channel after admitting his actions were unethical.

The footage showed players split into smaller groups carrying out different drills. On a separate full-size pitch, 11 players were positioned across the field alongside mannequins before gathering to receive instructions and feedback from new head coach Mauricio Pochettino.

Ten players were seen working with Pochettino in the video but only Harry Maguire, Kobbie Mainoo, Amad Diallo, Matheus Cunha and Bryan Mbeumo are regular starters among those.

Another group involved in drills was that of Lisandro Martinez, Matthijs de Ligt, Youri Tielemans, Mason Mount, Leny Yoro, Noussair Mazraoui and Diogo Dalot.

In his statement, the operator said the flight itself was legal under drone regulations but accepted that recording the training session was wrong.

He explained that his intention was to give Manchester United supporters their first look at Pochettino wearing the club’s training kit.

He said: “A total error of judgement on my part led to me filming some of the Manchester United training session at Carrington, whilst the flight was legal in terms of drone laws I fully accept it was unethical on my part.”

The operator apologized directly to the club, its new head coach and the players.

“I would like to apologise to Manchester United, Mauricio Pochettino and the players for my actions and sincerely hope those actions do not affect the outcome of any match.”

He also rejected any suggestion that Liverpool or another football club had been involved in his decision to film at Carrington.

“The video has been removed from my channel,” he added.

#2217. Jamie Carragher questions Mauricio Pochettino’s Manchester United appointment

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher believes Mauricio Pochettino has as much to prove as Manchester United, suggesting the Argentine’s reputation is not fully backed up by his achievements.

Mauricio Pochettino is preparing for his first match in charge of Manchester United against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday, following his departure from the United States national team to replace Michael Carrick.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea manager has arrived at Old Trafford with plenty of experience, but Carragher believes his appointment represents an opportunity for both United and Pochettino to rebuild their reputations.

Writing in The Telegraph, the former Liverpool defender highlighted the irony of United turning to a manager whose own career needs a fresh start.

“It is ironic that in their latest attempt to rejuvenate and restore some of their old fire, Manchester United have turned to a manager whose career needs a similar reawakening,” Carragher wrote.

He suggested that Pochettino should be grateful that a club of United’s stature came calling at this stage of his career.

“Manchester United need a savior to first ease them from the relegation zone and then revive their Premier League and Champions League credibility. Pochettino, meanwhile, needed a club like United to save him from a managerial career that promised more than it delivered.”

Manchester United were fortunate to secure a manager with Pochettino’s experience after dismissing Michael Carrick so early in the season. However, he believes the Argentine has plenty to prove despite his strong reputation in the game.

“The South American returns to England with a big reputation, but not enough on his CV to back up glowing testimonials,” he continued.

Carrick believes one of the biggest concerns surrounding Pochettino is his record in important matches.

“A recurring issue with Pochettino, which continued with the US team, is an inability to get over the line in the most consequential, winnable matches, resulting in a lack of big trophies.”

Despite questioning his ability to bottle in big games, he praised his teams when everything comes in together.

“Ten years ago his best team were the embodiment of him, full of energy, style and ambition. Time will tell whether this version of Pochettino is the one turning up at Old Trafford.”

Pochettino will have the chance to shut Carragher’s doubts on Sunday when Manchester United face Liverpool. His immediate priority will be to take United out of the relegation zone, but the expectations at Old Trafford will extend far beyond survival.

The Liverpool fixture will be his first opportunity to make an impression. Carragher, however, believes the Argentine’s real challenge is to turn his reputation into success.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino has made it clear he wants to be involved in Manchester United’s major squad decisions, while also addressing questions surrounding captain Bruno Fernandes ahead of his first game against Liverpool.

The new Manchester United head coach explained that he wants to have a voice in decisions that shape the squad.

“Sometimes maybe you are a coach and maybe you have more power in the decisions,” Pochettino said in his first Manchester United press conference.

“It depends on the structure, it depends on the communication, the relationships, you know, with all the areas.”

Mauricio Pochettino highlighted his responsibilities are clear about who he selects and plays in games.]

“I am the guy that is going to decide the squad for Sunday, and I’m going to pick the team, I’m going to make the changes, and of course in the future, I would like to be involved in all the main decisions that will happen on the squad.”

Pochettino was also asked about Bruno Fernandes, whose critical comments about Michael Carrick’s tactics became a talking point during the former manager’s final weeks at Old Trafford.

The Argentine was really keen about the squad’s commitment, dodging the blame on Fernandes and describing Fernandes as a key figure in the dressing room.

He is excited by the opportunity but knows there is little time to prepare for Sunday’s trip to Anfield.

“It’s a happy time but at the same time, so busy,” he explained.

“It’s been about adapting in the last few days, and it’s a race to arrive in condition to be competitive against Liverpool, and now our focus is 100 per cent on Liverpool in the game on Sunday.”

#2212. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.

#2211. Malo Gusto’s Kante Lesson Gives Chelsea A £75m Xabi Alonso Decision

Malo Gusto’s World Cup has suddenly become more than a France subplot for Chelsea. It is now a live assessment of whether Xabi Alonso inherits a right-back worth protecting, reshaping or trading at the peak of his market.

Chelsea’s own interview with Gusto this week underlined the human part of that equation. The 23-year-old spoke about learning from N’Golo Kante, calling the former Blue one of the best midfielders France have produced and stressing the value of his example on and off the pitch.

That matters because Kante is not just a nostalgic reference point. He remains the standard Chelsea supporters still use when judging humility, intensity and reliability in a high-pressure dressing room.

For Gusto, those qualities arrive at a decisive moment. Reports around the player’s future claim Chelsea value him at around £75million, with the imminent Marco Palestra deal creating a more crowded full-back picture.

That number looks aggressive, but the logic is clear. Gusto is young, capped by France, under contract, Premier League-tested and capable of playing in several defensive structures.

🚨🚨| NEW: Chelsea are open to selling Malo Gusto this summer, with his agent already in 𝐓𝐀𝐋𝐊𝐒 with Manchester City and Inter. Chelsea will only sell if their valuation is met.{@siphillipssport} pic.twitter.com/vNR2bWud9F

The easiest reading of Gusto’s Kante comments is sentimental. It should not be.

For Alonso, who officially begins work on July 1, the important detail is what those comments say about the player Chelsea have been developing.

Gusto has not always looked completely settled at Stamford Bridge. His best games have carried thrust, speed and recovery power, while his less convincing spells have raised questions about final-ball quality and defensive concentration.

That uncertainty is exactly why the next coach matters.

Alonso’s previous teams have leaned heavily on wide players who can receive under pressure, attack space early and still protect the back line when possession breaks down. Gusto has the athletic base for that job.

The test is whether his decision-making can be refined enough to make him a structural piece rather than a rotation asset.

That is where the Kante reference lands with force. Chelsea do not need Gusto to imitate Kante’s position. They need him to absorb the same professional standard: repeat actions, clean defensive choices, no wasted emotion and total commitment to the collective rhythm.

The Marco Palestra deal changes the right side of Chelsea’s squad immediately. Read Chelsea has already examined how Palestra’s arrival offers a tactical clue over Alonso’s defensive plans, and that pressure is now sharpened by market noise around Gusto.

Chelsea can frame the situation in two ways.

One is profit-led. If a club comes close to the reported £75million level, selling a player signed from Lyon for a much lower fee becomes a powerful PSR lever.

The other is football-led. If Alonso sees Gusto as a high-upside wing-back who fits his model, selling him just as the coaching environment becomes more suitable would carry long-term risk.

The complication is Reece James. The captain’s fitness issues, including his current England hamstring concern, mean Chelsea still need genuine right-sided depth.

Palestra may be part of the future, but Gusto is already part of the present. Chelsea have too often needed emergency solutions in that area to treat proven depth lightly.

That is why Read Chelsea’s recent look at James awaiting hamstring scan results matters in this debate. Right-back remains one of the most talented areas of the squad, but also one of the least straightforward.

This is why Gusto’s France rhythm is more valuable than a simple tournament diary entry. Chelsea are watching a player operate inside an elite international camp, learn from a club legend and handle a public transfer valuation that can change his status overnight.

Alonso’s first decision does not have to be final, but it does have to be clear.

If Gusto stays, he needs a defined role early in pre-season and a pathway that makes sense alongside Palestra and James. If Chelsea sell, they need to do it at a number that justifies weakening one of the few squad areas where youth, speed and top-level experience already overlap.

Gusto’s Kante lesson, then, is not just a feel-good Chelsea connection. It is a reminder of the standard Alonso must demand before deciding whether £75million is a price to accept or a warning sign that Chelsea should hold firm.

#2210. Senegal Knockout Tie Gives Chelsea A Double Xabi Alonso Audit

Chelsea’s World Cup watch has moved beyond simple minutes management.

Senegal’s Round-of-32 meeting with Belgium now gives Xabi Alonso a live read on two very different development tracks.

Nicolas Jackson’s attacking authority is the immediate question. Mamadou Sarr’s defensive readiness is the longer-term one.

Chelsea confirmed that Senegal kept their knockout hopes alive with a 5-0 win over Iraq, with Jackson featuring from the bench.

Senegal have since moved into the knockouts, where they face Belgium in Seattle on Wednesday 1 July.

Jackson and Sarr travel into that game with contrasting club questions attached. Both still carry clear Chelsea relevance.

Sarr has not been the loudest Chelsea story of the tournament.

That may actually make this test more useful.

The 20-year-old is still moving through the awkward stage between prospect, squad option and saleable BlueCo asset.

Knockout football against Belgium can sharpen that picture quickly.

The timing is awkward in the best possible way for Chelsea. Alonso steps into a squad with expensive centre-backs, academy-trained options and a board still comfortable using the market aggressively.

A disciplined Sarr performance would not end that search. It would give the recruitment team a stronger internal benchmark before committing another major fee.

Chelsea need defenders who can hold a high line, cover wide channels and still pass through pressure. Belgium should ask those questions more directly than most group-stage opponents.

The context matters. Chelsea’s recall note recorded that Sarr returned from Strasbourg after 18 appearances across Ligue 1 and the Conference League.

The club also noted that he had already earned his first Senegal cap.

That is a different profile from an academy player being carried by reputation alone.

There is a statistical base behind the interest too. When Chelsea completed the deal, beIN SPORTS noted that Sarr made 27 Ligue 1 appearances in his first Strasbourg season.

He helped them keep 10 clean sheets. He also produced a team-high 20 blocks and made 25 interceptions.

Those numbers explain why this Belgium game is not just a sentimental World Cup subplot.

Alonso’s Chelsea defence is already crowded with Levi Colwill, Wesley Fofana, Trevoh Chalobah and external targets still orbiting the plan.

ReadChelsea has already looked at why Colwill’s recovery story gives Alonso a defensive test. Sarr’s value is different.

He is still being evaluated as a physical, long-range defensive project.

Jackson’s role changes the editorial weight of the tie.

Chelsea’s striker picture has been messy for long enough. Any high-pressure international performance now carries wider meaning.

ReadChelsea has already framed the forward’s tournament as a live Senegal striker audit for Chelsea.

Belgium should stress both Chelsea questions at once.

Jackson has to offer penalty-box threat and transition security. Sarr, if trusted, has to defend space against a technically cleaner opponent.

He also has to show whether his recovery pace and frame translate under tournament pressure.

Sarr’s own mentality profile is worth noting. In a Chelsea World Cup Q&A, he named Lionel Messi, Sadio Mane and Paul Pogba as his tournament heroes.

His answer on Pogba focused on leadership and group mentality.

That lands neatly with the exact question Chelsea now need answered. Is there enough presence to go with the physical tools?

This is why Belgium feels like a pathway check rather than a routine World Cup watch item.

Chelsea do not need Sarr to solve the defence in July. They need clearer evidence on whether he belongs in Alonso’s first pre-season group.

He could need another loan. He could also become part of the club’s centre-back market arithmetic.

For Jackson, the question is more immediate. For Sarr, it is more structural.

Together, Senegal’s knockout tie gives Chelsea a rare clean look at two players on opposite ends of the same squad-building argument.

Who can help now, and who is worth protecting for later?

#2209. Liverpool games v Man United and Everton moved as Premier League confirm updated fixture details

Two more Liverpool fixtures in the Premier League have been rescheduled for live TV coverage, as confirmed on Thursday.

With the November international break now concluded, the Reds’ next couple of domestic fixtures are against Manchester United and Everton, with the reverse of those games against our two biggest traditional rivals taking place on consecutive weekends in January.

The fixture list for the remainder of this year had already been locked in, and the first month of 2027 has now been set, other than prospective FA Cup and Carabao Cup matches.

On Thursday, the Premier League announced updated dates and times for matches in January, with two of Liverpool’s games being moved.

The home clash against Crystal Palace remains a 3pm kick-off on Saturday 16th, but the subsequent two games have unsurprisingly been chosen for TV coverage.

The Reds will now face Manchester United at Old Trafford on Sunday 24 January at 4:30pm (live on Sky Sports), with the Merseyside derby at Anfield taking place six days later with a 12:30pm kick-off (live on TNT Sports).

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

The new dates and times for the Man United and Everton games have left Liverpool with a rather difficult week towards the end of January.

Just three days after the game at Old Trafford, the Reds have their concluding fixture in the league phase of the Champions League at home to Lens, and then a turnaround of just over 50 hours to hosting the Toffees at Anfield.

*FA Cup third round draw and fixture details to be confirmed in December

**If Liverpool advance to Carabao Cup semi-finals

The hope is that, by the time the Ligue 1 outfit visit L4 on 27 January, we’ll already be assured of a direct passage to the round of 16 and there won’t be anything riding on that game for Andoni Iraola’s side.

Liverpool currently have seven matches in the diary in the first month of 2027, with an FA Cup third-round tie to be added for the weekend of 9/10 January and a potential Carabao Cup semi-final first leg in the subsequent midweek, should we defeat Bournemouth at Anfield next month.

It’s quite plausible that the Reds could have two matches against Everton in January, with both teams still in the Carabao Cup and favourites to win their respective quarter-finals (the Toffees are away to Fleetwood of League Two).

Three Merseyside derbies in the first five weeks of 2027 would be the definition of familiarity breeding contempt, but for now the sole focus for Iraola and co is the small matter of Man United at home on Sunday.
