#2208. Michael Olise Gives Chelsea A Hard Xabi Alonso Wide-Player Benchmark

Michael Olise has moved from former Chelsea academy footnote to genuine Ballon d’Or conversation piece.

That should land at Cobham as more than a wistful what-if.

Bavarian Football Works relayed comments from former Chelsea and France winger Florent Malouda, who described the Bayern Munich forward as a live Ballon d’Or contender if France turn their World Cup run into silverware.

The point is not simply that Chelsea once had Olise in the building. It is that his rise provides a hard benchmark for the type of final-third specialist Xabi Alonso must either develop or buy.

Chelsea have spent heavily on attacking upside. Olise’s rise is a reminder that the real separator is not potential, but repeatable elite production.

Bayern’s own season review underlines why the conversation has accelerated.

The German champions confirmed Olise finished 2025/26 with 25 goals and 28 assists from 57 competitive appearances. He was also named Bayern’s Player of the Season for a second straight year, finishing ahead of Harry Kane and Luis Diaz in the fan vote.

That is elite production from a wide creator who does not play like a chalk-on-boots winger.

Olise can receive wide, drift inside, manipulate the half-space and still deliver the last pass at match-winning speed. Chelsea have bought attackers with pieces of that profile, but few have joined the dots with that level of consistency.

There is an obvious recruitment lesson here.

The market will now price that blend of creativity, ball security and goal threat close to superstar territory. If Chelsea want an Alonso attack that controls games rather than simply runs through them, the next winger decision cannot be judged on pace or upside alone.

It has to be judged on game control, final-third calm and repeat output.

The World Cup has sharpened the comparison.

Chelsea’s official France preview noted that Malo Gusto was in the same squad as N’Golo Kante and former Blues academy player Olise. It was a neat reminder of how many Chelsea strands sit inside Didier Deschamps’ group.

Bundesliga’s tournament analysis was even more revealing. It noted that Olise had reached 10 France goal involvements in 18 appearances and was named Player of the Match on his World Cup debut against Senegal.

That same analysis highlighted the tactical adjustment that moved Olise closer to Kylian Mbappe after half-time. His passing range then became the lever that changed the game.

That matters for Chelsea because Alonso’s best Bayer Leverkusen sides were built around players who could receive between lines without killing the tempo. Cole Palmer already gives Chelsea one version of that.

The wider question is whether the squad has another attacker who can bend matches from the opposite side without turning every possession into a one-v-one.

Olise’s Chelsea connection can easily become sentimental. The sharper editorial point is structural.

This is the calibre of player who once passed through the academy pathway and later became a global-level attacker elsewhere. Chelsea cannot change that history, but they can use it to stress-test the next wave of decisions.

That matters for Tyrique George, Kendry Paez, Estevao and any new wide signing.

ReadChelsea has already looked at how Tyrique George’s likely Everton return gives Alonso a fresh winger audit. Olise’s current standing raises the standard of that discussion.

He is no longer just a former academy name or a missed Premier League target. He is evidence of what happens when technical security, role clarity and patience meet elite coaching.

For Alonso, that is the real value of the Olise noise.

Chelsea do not need to turn every academy exit into regret. They do need to recognise the standard now being set by one of Cobham’s former pupils.

The next wide forward who gets serious minutes at Stamford Bridge has to be assessed against that ceiling, not against the safer language of potential.

#2207. Mikel Merino speaks out on Arsenal role as January rumours grow

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Merino has played down any speculation around his potential exit during January. He has made it clear he will fight to keep his place in the squad.

He also spoke about speculation around Martin Zubimendi, who has been linked with Atletico Madrid.

Both players have been questioned over their role in Mikel Arteta’s midfield. There is increasing competition for positions at the Emirates.

Merino feels that there is always an intense fight for places in top-level football. He spoke about how strong and deep Arsenal are ahead of their Nations League clash against the Czech Republic.

Both Merino and Zubimendi were also keen to stress that they will be looking to gain more opportunities and not get distracted.

Merino has had to adapt to his new role at Arsenal after playing a key role on his team’s side. However, Merino has struggled to get into the starting XI.

He admitted that he wants to play more games. However, he acknowledged that it was difficult to get into the team, as there are so many quality players.

This shows that his playing time has not affected his relationship with Arteta.

Merino was able to understand what it is like to be a manager because his father was a football coach. He understands that tough decisions have to be made when selecting your starting XI.

This can be an important aspect in a difficult season, as all players need to play their part to make sure Arsenal stay challenging in multiple competitions.

Merino’s comments are very interesting considering the uncertainty around Zubimendi.

There are reports Zubimendi has been linked to Atletico Madrid and whether he will be looking to move in January.

Merino didn’t give a definite answer that these players will stay at Arsenal, but the comments do suggest they are looking to improve their position in the squad.

He also underlined the importance of having good cover, with Spain boss Luis de la Fuente believing players coming off the bench can be just as vital as those starting.

This is something Arsenal will want their squad to take on board. They will not always be able to play the same midfielders due to the demands of playing in both domestic and European competitions.

There is still time for Merino and Zubimendi to turn their situation around, so what they are doing now may not indicate how important they are going to be in the campaign.

Arsenal will be keen to keep Merino happy, and he has made his position clear. This should give some reassurance ahead of the January transfer window.

#2206. Chelsea Could Claim Share of £164m Compensation

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Chelsea fans have followed Manchester City’s financial rules case for years. Everyone at Stamford Bridge knows a guilty verdict could cost City serious money, and much of it could end up with rivals lower down the table.

Reports now suggest an independent panel has reached a decision. If that holds up, the scale of any Chelsea Manchester City compensation payout could run into tens of millions of pounds for the Blues alone.

The Premier League charged Manchester City with 115 alleged breaches of financial regulations in February 2023. That followed a four-year investigation into the club’s accounts. The case has hung over the competition ever since. Every top-flight club knows the outcome could change how recent Premier League seasons are remembered.

This matters directly to Chelsea. If City are found to have broken spending and disclosure rules, clubs who finished below them could argue they lost out on prize money and European qualification. Chelsea have competed directly with City for top four places and cup competitions throughout that period.

According to The Athletic, Manchester City were found guilty on all but one of the 115 charges by the independent panel. The Premier League has not confirmed the verdict or issued a statement.

Manchester City have not accepted any suggestion of guilt. The club released a statement addressing the ongoing process directly.

“The Premier League process remains ongoing, with significant elements to be completed, and subject to strict confidentiality. As such, Manchester City FC’s position remains consistent with the Club’s statement of February 2023. ‘The Club has diligently respected due process for eight years on the basis that the Premier League Board and Executive would behave as an independent, impartial and fair-minded regulator, free from partisan influence.'”

That statement makes clear the club does not see the case as settled. Until the Premier League confirms a result, the reported verdict will linger on for official sentencing.

Data specialists at OLBG have estimated the potential Chelsea Manchester City compensation windfall if City are found guilty. They believe City could owe domestic rivals well over £500m in lost Premier League prize money if the charges are proven.

Within that total, London clubs are said to be due around £164m between them. Chelsea, Arsenal and Tottenham Hotspur are among the clubs who could be entitled to a share. Other Premier League sides could benefit too, depending on where they finished compared with City in the seasons in question.

Prize money in the Premier League is tied closely to league position. If City gained points unfairly, that would have pushed a rival down the table and cost them money too. That is the basis for the OLBG figures, and it explains why the sums involved are so large.

For Chelsea, even a share of that £164m pot would be a big boost. It could help fund new signings, or simply ease the pressure around the kind of transfer valuations at Stamford Bridge the club faces every window.

City chairman Khaldoon Al Mubarak has already made clear the case is far from finished. He described the process as having “a long way to run.” That points to appeals and further legal steps, whatever the panel has decided.

Chelsea, for their part, carry on with life under Xabi Alonso regardless of how the case unfolds. Attention stays on squad rotation and Xabi Alonso’s squad management strategy, not on any potential windfall. The financial case will not change anything on the pitch in the short term.

The Premier League has stayed silent on the reported verdict, and confidentiality remains strict around the process, according to reports from Football.London. Chelsea fans should expect a long wait before any compensation, if it comes at all, reaches the club’s accounts.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2205. Arsenal Women vs Barcelona: Arsenal show their resilience with impressive draw against Barcelona

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal Women vs Barcelona: Arsenal Women claimed a vital point at the Camp Nou. They drew 1-1 against Barcelona in the Champions League on Thursday night.

Frida Maanum gave the Gunners the lead in the first half. Caroline Graham Hansen scored to bring the game level after the break. Whilst Renee Slegers will be disappointed to miss out on all three points, her side played some encouraging football.

Despite needing to play differently, Arsenal had an even game against an opposition who would inevitably have long periods of the ball. The visitors played very well defensively and looked dangerous when they could attack.

The result showed that Slegers has a team that can adapt to new challenges. This could be important for Arsenal as they try to make a mark in Europe.

One of the key decisions made by Slegers was to include Maanum. She had only recently returned from injury but made an impressive impact against Barcelona.

The midfielder scored early on to start the game in the right direction. Slegers was very impressed with her play when not in possession. She was praised for her communication skills and assisting others.

Arsenal’s attacking play was mainly based on fast counter attacks. They knew they wouldn’t be able to play in the same style as Barcelona and therefore tried to take advantage of the space on offer.

However, there were chances created in both halves. Arsenal may have been more clinical with their chances, they were always a threat.

Eventually, Barcelona scored their equaliser through Graham Hansen. Slegers said that Arsenal lost their shape during the move, which gave Hansen too much space.

It was a reminder of how quickly Barcelona can strike if you lose your concentration for a moment.

The Arsenal manager was pleased with the way her players implemented the game plan, showing tenacity, discipline, good communication and bravery.

Slegers admitted Arsenal did lose the ball too much in the second half, and by keeping it longer, they would have taken the pressure off defending against Barca’s dangerous attack.

There was some frustration that Arsenal couldn’t hold onto their lead, and there were more chances created towards the end of the game. The visitors got into good positions to win corners but couldn’t get the winning goal.

They will feel very positive from their display. They put themselves up against one of the biggest tests in the Camp Nou and proved that they can cause Barcelona problems.

There isn’t much time to really reflect on the result ahead of their game against the London City Lionesses on Sunday, but Slegers now has another example of what her side can do when they play with purpose and structure.

#2203. Jamie Gittens suffers Chelsea injury blow with England U21s

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Gittens has withdrawn from the England Under-21 squad. He suffered an ankle injury during Tuesday’s qualifier against Kazakhstan at Molineux. The Chelsea winger left the ground on crutches. He has returned to Stamford Bridge, where the Jamie Gittens injury will now be assessed by the club’s medical staff.

Gittens picked up the knock after a challenge during the second half of England’s win over Kazakhstan. He was withdrawn as a precaution. He did not take any further part in the match. England have been careful not to overstate the issue and no further reports have come out further.

The 21-year-old struggled to put weight on the affected ankle and was seen limping around Molineux after full time. That prompted an immediate release back to his club, rather than any attempt to assess him further in camp.

England Under-21 boss Lee Carsley addressed the injury directly after the game. He confirmed the nature of the knock and the decision to send Gittens home.

“Jamie got a kick on his ankle,” Carsley said after the match. “We never take any risks. With the age of these players, we don’t ever keep them in camp to see how they are. He will go back.”

England’s statement on the squad changes, detailed in a report from Football.London, confirmed the wider fallout from the fixture.

“Mukasa’s call-up follows the withdrawal of Chelsea’s Jamie Gittens and Norwich City pair Kellen Fisher and Ben Chrisene through injury,” the statement read. West Ham midfielder Divine Mukasa has been called up as cover for all three.

Gittens has been an unused substitute in every Premier League fixture Chelsea have played this season, with his only two appearances under Xabi Alonso coming in the Carabao Cup. Those minutes have offered him little platform after his big summer move.

Alonso’s squad selection has been a talking point since he took charge. Our earlier look at Xabi Alonso’s squad choices at Stamford Bridge covered how competitive things have become. An ankle injury will not change his pecking order in the short term. It does, though, remove any chance of him pushing his case this week.

Chelsea’s break has brought other headlines too. Cole Palmer also withdrew, for entirely different reasons, after his recall by senior boss Thomas Tuchel to the full England squad. Chelsea will be watching both returns closely before domestic football resumes.

Chelsea have not yet issued a formal update on the severity of the injury. Any recovery timeline will depend on an assessment by the club’s medical team once Gittens is back at Cobham. Nothing beyond the crutches seen at Molineux has been confirmed.

Given his limited game time this season, the immediate concern is less about missed fixtures than a setback to his integration under Alonso. Fans can follow how the squad shapes up before the next fixtures through our recent line-up updates. Further news on Gittens is expected once Chelsea’s medical staff finish their evaluation.

#2201. Manu Koné: Roma slap £86m price tag on Chelsea target

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Roma have put an £86m (€100m) price tag on Manu Koné, according to Gazzetta dello Sport, reviving the Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link that first emerged over the summer. Roma want any interested club, including Chelsea, to know the price before serious talks can even begin.

Koné remains under contract at the Stadio Olimpico, and he has become a key part of Roma’s midfield since his move from Borussia Mönchengladbach. The Gazzetta report reads like a warning to potential buyers rather than proof of any talks between the two clubs.

Roma sporting director Tony D’Amico has confirmed Chelsea made contact late in the summer window, though he was quick to draw a clear line under how far things went.

“There was an inquiry and some preliminary contact with Chelsea on the final day of the transfer window. No official offers arrived that would allow us to approach the player. Something was imagined that simply didn’t happen. There was no official offer; the final day of the transfer window got everyone a little worked up.”

D’Amico’s comments confirm Chelsea looked at signing Koné before the deadline, but they stop well short of showing an official bid was ever made. He also explained why Roma had no wish to sell a key player so close to the window closing.

“Before the match against Fiorentina (on 24 August), I said it would be difficult for us to deprive ourselves of important players so near to the end of the transfer session, and so it proved. Kone is an important player, we’re happy he stayed with us, and we’re confident he’ll give his contribution during the season.”

Chelsea’s midfield has changed shape under Xabi Alonso since the summer, with Morgan Rogers arriving to add depth and competition for places. That signing came during a window that also saw exits acceleration under Xabi Alonso, as the head coach reshaped his squad around his own style.

Koné’s ball winning and his range of progressive passing would suit Alonso well, should Chelsea ever revisit their interest. Supporters have already seen how Alonso rotates his midfield for the biggest games, including the recent derby line-up selections against Brentford.

For now, Rogers and Chelsea’s existing central options give Alonso enough cover without needing an immediate signing. Roma’s reluctance to sell also means any deal would need patience rather than a quick resolution.

Nothing in D’Amico’s comments suggests Roma are open to a sale in the near term, and Koné has given no sign that he wants to leave. The £86m valuation, reported by Football London, stands as Roma’s marker rather than an asking price involved with Chelsea.

Will Chelsea return with a more formal approach in a future window? D’Amico’s comments give no hint either way. The Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link is unlikely to fade given Chelsea’s history of scouting France’s leading midfielders, but any fresh move will have to wait for Roma’s stance to soften.

#2200. Why England fans booed Arsenal defender Ben White again after Croatia win

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Ben White returned to the England senior squad on Thursday night but received a hostile reception at Wembley.

The Arsenal defender came on as a substitute in the 78th minute of England’s 2-1 Nations League win against Croatia but was met with boos from a section of the crowd.

Thomas Tuchel has since defended White, stating that he should be in the squad. However, this raises questions as to why some England supporters have turned on the defender after he has continued to perform well for Arsenal.

The answer lies with the 2022 World Cup in Qatar.

He did not play in the first two group games for England and missed the last group game against Wales due to illness.

There were reports that White had fallen out with assistant manager Steve Holland, but neither the FA nor England management confirmed this was the reason he left.

Gareth Southgate later stated there were no problems between White and Holland and that White had simply withdrawn himself from selection after the tournament.

This meant that White would never play for England again while Southgate was still manager. Some fans were clearly upset by this, as they felt White was shunning his country.

When White returned under Tuchel in March 2026, he was met with a negative reaction. He was booed by the crowd when he scored his first-ever international goal against Uruguay. White was also booed in the same fashion against Japan.

He has since explained that supporters might view the situation differently if they knew the full story. However, he has chosen not to reveal the private details behind his departure.

Tuchel has shown little concern about White being affected by the controversy.

The England boss said after the win on Thursday that he had not heard any boos and praised White’s qualities, work ethic and desire to play for his country.

He believes White can help England win games and added that he has the backing of the coaching staff.

Arsenal fans will be unsurprised to learn that White is still a valuable asset for Mikel Arteta, offering defensive consistency and being able to play as a right or centre-back.

The bad press that White is receiving from England fans will not go away any time soon. However, he has not done anything to hurt his reputation at club level. The reasons why he left for the World Cup are personal.

The main focus for Arsenal will be getting him match fit again. As for the England fans, we shall see.

#2199. Arsenal Women vs London City Lionesses preview: Gunners face tough test at the Emirates

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal look to build on their recent win over Liverpool as they return to the Emirates Stadium but will face an impressive London City Lionesses side who are a potent attacking threat on their travels.

Renée Slegers’ team have struggled to convert their strong performances into points in the league this season. They have lost their last four WSL games at home, however, becoming the first team in WSL history to draw four successive games at home.

Their bad run must come to an end. They have taken just three points from their first nine league games so far this season, scoring a miserable total of 10 goals. However, they look like they have more in the tank and will need to be much more clinical in front of the goal.

The day also marks Slegers’ 50th game in charge of the WSL. The Dutch manager has only lost six league games so far this season, with only Chelsea having a better record from teams that have been ever-present in the division.

Arsenal can’t take their opposition lightly after London City’s impressive recent form. They have won two and drawn once in their last three away league games, scoring 12 goals.

Eder Maestre’s side has become a potent attacking force. Daniëlle van de Donk has provided four assists this season, and Delphine Cascarino has scored three goals and provided two assists.

Kadidiatou Diani can be dangerous. She has scored or assisted in all four of her league games away from home this season.

London City’s attacking prowess means they are a far different proposition to some teams Arsenal have faced at home. They have been in a positive situation for 62.7 per cent of their league minutes this season, the highest in the WSL.

Arsenal will have to deal with the attacking threats but look to utilise their ball possession better. Giving London City an opportunity to counter could make an already tough afternoon even tougher.

Alessia Russo comes into the game having found the net in her last three WSL games and will be key in converting Arsenal’s chances into goals.

The Gunners also showed their fighting spirit in their midweek clash against Barcelona. They drew 1-1 at Camp Nou to become the first side to take points off the Spanish giants in the Women’s Champions League at home since April 2024.

Slegers’ team will feel emboldened by that result but will have to change their tactics against London City.

They have won all seven games against Arsenal so far, scoring an impressive 22 goals and only conceding once. However, given London City’s recent away form, they will be hotly favoured.

They certainly have the quality to take all three points, but they must finally convert their dominance into a comfortable home win.

Cissé has already hit the ground running in Italy and now needs to prove that his initial success can turn into something more consistent.

#2196. Pep Guardiola Speaks Out as Man City Found Guilty of 114 Charges

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester City have been found guilty of 114 financial charges, the Premier League has confirmed. The verdict follows an eight-week hearing that opened almost two years ago. It closes one of the most significant disciplinary cases in the competition’s history.

Former head coach Pep Guardiola has already broken his silence. Reaction to the Pep Guardiola Man City charges verdict is spreading fast across the division, with Chelsea among the clubs watching closely.

Guardiola joined Manchester City in 2016 and turned them into the dominant force in English football. He left his post earlier this summer, with the club having already worked through plans to replace Pep Guardiola at City.

His exit means he holds no formal role at the Etihad. Yet he moved quickly to make his loyalty clear once the ruling landed. According to the football.london report that first carried his statement, Guardiola posted directly to his supporters.

“I want every single @ManCity fan to know I am here; more than ever. I am, always have been, always will, be behind my club, my owner, my chairman [Khaldoon Al Mubarak], Ferran [Soriano], the players, staff, Enzo [Maresca] and you, our unique supporters. Let me be clear: we will get through this together. I love you all.”

Manchester City chief executive Ferran Soriano has confirmed that the club intends to appeal the verdict. The statement from Guardiola comes to City’s defence despite him leaving the role this summer. He is still viewed as the man who built this era of success, even though he no longer sits in the dugout at the Etihad.

The Pep Guardiola Man City charges case matters just as much to Chelsea as it does to Manchester City themselves. Chelsea, along with Arsenal and Tottenham, are among the Premier League clubs directly affected by the outcome. Every point City may have gained through breaches has a knock on effect further down the table.

Most top flight clubs including Chelsea are now pushing for City to face relegation as a consequence of the verdict. Some are reported to be weighing up legal action of their own. Guardiola has spoken about Chelsea before, including Guardiola’s previous comments on Chelsea. This latest statement fits a pattern of public backing for Manchester City, despite the criticism now directed at the club.

For Chelsea supporters, the case is not just about points on a table. It touches on the integrity of seasons they competed in directly. That is why so many rivals are refusing to let the matter rest with a guilty verdict alone.

Manchester City’s appeal process is now the next stage in a case that has already run for nearly two years. Ferran Soriano has made clear the club will contest the findings. No date has been confirmed for when that appeal will be heard.

Until then, no points deductions, penalties or changes to past titles have been confirmed by the Premier League. Chelsea and their rivals will be watching the appeal timeline just as closely as they watched the original hearing. The final outcome could still be a long way off.

#2195. Max Dowman breaks Jude Bellingham record as Arsenal teenager makes England history

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Max Dowman has added another incredible achievement to his burgeoning career after becoming the youngest player to play for the England Under-21s.

The Arsenal youngster was introduced from the bench in Friday’s 1-0 friendly loss to Portugal at Ashton Gate. The 16-year-10-month-13-day-old player is over three months younger than Jude Bellingham was when he played his first game for the Under-21s.

Dowman replaced former Arsenal academy player Amario Cozier-Duberry in the 61st minute. He came on alongside debutant Trey Nyoni as England tried to shift the momentum of a challenging game.

An early strike from Afonso Moreira gave Portugal the lead in the 28th minute. An ineffective England team failed to respond. However, the replacements gave Lee Carsley’s team some late momentum.

Despite the hosts’ attempts to level the score, they failed to break down the Portuguese defence. The result means England have lost for the second game running. However, Dowman had much to be pleased with personally.

Dowman has added to his growing collection of records.

He became the youngest player to play in the Champions League, the youngest goalscorer in the history of the Premier League and the youngest to win the Premier League title.

Breaking Bellingham’s England Under-21 record is another significant step. It shows how quickly Dowman has progressed through the international age groups.

He has already made a big impression at Arsenal with Mikel Arteta’s love of giving younger players a chance, but Dowman will have some stiff competition for first team minutes.

Another positive is his progression on an international scale. He will improve both physically and tactically playing against older players and gain experience at a higher level.

England will host France in their next friendly on Monday, 16th November. Dowman will be hoping to feature after his instant impact on the record books.

Despite the disappointing result against Portugal, it was a landmark evening for the Arsenal players.

Dowman is already building a legacy at club level, but now he can add to his list of records by becoming an England Under-21 record holder.

At just 16, he has plenty of time to improve and show consistent performances to get more opportunities with the senior Arsenal squad.

#2194. Alex Scott Chelsea Transfer Blocked as Bournemouth Eye January Stay

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bournemouth will block any Alex Scott Chelsea transfer in January. That is according to reports from the Daily Mail and BBC Sport. Both outlets say the Cherries want to keep their young midfielder as they fight to protect their Premier League status this season.

Chelsea have been linked with serious interest in the 22-year-old. Reports suggest the Blues have already tested Bournemouth’s resolve with a bid worth close to £64 million. Bournemouth have given no indication they are willing to negotiate. Scott, the reports say, simply is not for sale this winter.

Reports in the Daily Mail and on BBC Sport state that Bournemouth have no intention of sanctioning a sale this winter. The club’s hierarchy reportedly view Scott as central to their survival plan, wary of leaning on new signings mid-season rather than trusting the squad they already have.

A fee of around £64 million, first reported by Football London, is said to have been offered by Chelsea during recent discussions.

Scott joined Bournemouth from Bristol City in 2023. He has quickly become one of the most sought-after young midfielders in English football. Interest from a club the size of Chelsea shows how far his reputation has grown, though Bournemouth’s stance makes a January move increasingly unlikely.

Scott’s rise has been underlined by his first appearance for England. He came off the bench for a 20-minute cameo as Thomas Tuchel’s side beat Czechia 2-0 in the Nations League. The manager praised him afterwards.

“Alex Scott has maybe one of the best performances in midfield in the Premier League since the start with Bournemouth.”

“Individually he’s one of the outstanding midfielders so he clearly deserves to be with us.”

“He took another step into the right direction. Very impressive and happy that he is on that route. Now he plays in Europe for Bournemouth and deserves to be with us.”

That praise from their former manager will only increase Chelsea’s interest. Scott’s energy and composure suit the identity Xabi Alonso wants to build at Stamford Bridge. The Spaniard has already shown he is willing to rotate his squad, as seen in Xabi Alonso’s squad management decisions this season.

Chelsea travel to face Bournemouth in the Premier League soon. It is a fixture that will give Alonso a close look at a player repeatedly linked with his club. Build-up to that game is already underway, with team news arriving ahead of the Bournemouth clash.

The prospects of an Alex Scott Chelsea transfer happening in January now look slim. Supporters hoping for fresh competition in central midfield may need patience, with Chelsea more likely to revisit their interest when the summer window opens.

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Martin Ødegaard has spoken out about the speculation surrounding his future. He has stated he would like to stay at Arsenal for the future.

The captain has also admitted that talks about a new deal have not yet begun. Ødegaard is currently under contract until the summer of 2028. This leaves Arsenal with an important decision to make over the coming months.

Bayern Munich are being linked with an interest in the midfielder according to reports from Germany. The Bundesliga giants are said to be keeping their eyes on the player’s situation.

Speaking while on international duty with Norway, Ødegaard was quick to dismiss the speculation. He made it clear he is settled in north London and sees his future being at the Emirates Stadium.

There is no suggestion that the 27-year-old is seeking a move. Although comments do highlight a contract issue that Arsenal will be keen to resolve in the short term.

The Norwegian’s laid-back attitude should be encouraging for supporters.

He has made it clear that he is happy at the club and wants to stay here for the time being. This means that there is no need to panic.

However, Arsenal will have to get him to sign on the dotted line. Leaving his captain in the last year of his contract with no progress would cause an unnecessary distraction.

Since his permanent move from Real Madrid in 2021, Ødegaard has become a key player in Mikel Arteta’s plans. He has grown into a team leader and is one of the most influential players in the dressing room.

His importance has been shown by his inclusion in all 10 of Arsenal’s Premier League games so far this season. He has scored three goals.

It wouldn’t be a shock to see Bayern Munich show interest in Ødegaard, but don’t take this as a sign he is close to a move.

There is little reason for Arsenal to consider losing their captain. He brings so much more to the team than just his technical ability and has been relied upon by Arteta to lead by example.

The midfielder was also pivotal in the 1-0 win over Hull City on 7 November, scoring the winner in the 72nd minute to give the league leaders three crucial points.

Arsenal have put themselves in a good position in the title race. Keeping their key players happy will be important in the second half of the campaign.

The Gunners will be focused on their game against Newcastle United on 21st November.

Arsenal fans have no real reason to panic at the moment, with the captain wanting to stay at the club and his comments suggesting he is happy.

However, it would be a good idea for the club to try and ensure his long-term future at the club. Ødegaard is still an important part of Arteta’s strategy, and ensuring he stays at the club would be a smart move.

#2191. Arsenal Women held by London City Lionesses despite Frida Maanum goal

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal Women missed the chance to end their frustrating run of draws at home as London City Lionesses came from behind to earn a 1-1 draw at the Emirates Stadium.

Frida Maanum gave the Gunners the lead in an exciting start to the game. But Isobel Goodwin equalised almost straight away. Despite creating plenty of chances, Renée Slegers’ side failed to find a second goal.

Arsenal have now lost their last five WSL games at the Emirates. The results were yet another frustrating afternoon for the defending champions, who put in a strong display against Barcelona in Europe earlier in the week.

Arsenal started on a high note, creating some dangerous chances with their quick play. Emily Fox and Lisa Baum worked really well together down the right, and Ona Batlle had a great chance from the edge of the box.

As the pressure mounted, Fox surged forward and played a perfectly weighted through ball to Alessia Russo. The striker turned and played the ball back to Maanum, who rounded off her impressive comeback from injury by finishing.

Arsenal had little time to rejoice. London City equalised just a minute later as Goodwin found herself in space to fire past Misa Rodríguez.

The visitors looked more confident after equalising, but Mary Earps was proving a tough ask for Arsenal to score past. The ex-Manchester United shot-stopper kept out Baum and made some key stops from Arsenal’s efforts.

Rodriguez also had to make a key stop before half-time as she got a hand to Daniela Arques Lazaro’s powerful strike to keep the scores level.

The Gunners continued their attacking play but were made to work hard by a high pressing side from London City.

But Arsenal still managed to create some chances. Caitlin Foord put Earps under pressure and Maanum and Baum combined before the latter saw her curl just wide.

London City posed a threat too. With Kadidiatou Diani and Grace Geyoro causing defensive problems for Arsenal, Geyoro fired into the side netting.

Delphine Cascarino had the game’s defining moment of the second half, hitting the crossbar from outside the box. However, Arsenal were lucky to escape and Maanum came close to winning the game, hitting the woodwork from a dangerous counter.

Slegers brought on Kyra Cooney-Cross and Olivia Smith in the 72nd minute to provide some new energy. Smith looked lively upon entering the game, but Earps denied her efforts and Batlle’s cross.

Emily Fox had to be substituted due to injury in the final moments of the game, but Slegers later indicated that she would be okay.

The Gunners were unable to find a winning goal and will now turn their attention to their Champions League clash on Thursday with Lyon. They will then face West Ham at home in the WSL.

#2190. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

#2189. Declan Rice’s latest Arsenal evolution shows why Arteta wanted him tied down

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Declan Rice’s new contract shows how much of an asset he is to Mikel Arteta. His recent performances show why.

The England international has reportedly signed a new five-year deal which will keep him at the Emirates Stadium until 2031. The contract is a reward for the midfielder who has been instrumental in Arsenal’s success since he moved to the club in 2023 from West Ham.

Rice has not just provided the presence and protection that Arsenal were hoping for with their £105 million signing. He has continued to develop his game, especially after returning to his deeper midfield role this season.

Rice came in as a defensive midfielder, but Arteta quickly saw how much he could offer higher up the park.

His physicality, ability to run into the box and set piece threat made him a useful left-sided number eight. Rice had a huge impact in midfield for Arsenal, bringing goals and a new attacking threat.

But Rice has played in a more advanced position on the right-hand side of the midfield duo this season, and this has not limited his impact but instead highlighted another key part of his game.

Rice has made the most line-breaking passes in the Premier League this season, with Opta stats reported by Sky Sports showing he is second only to Premier League players.

This is of particular interest, as he has often come under criticism for his distribution. But he is averaging nearly double the amount of line-breaking passes he did last season.

While his running ability is still an asset, he is also becoming much better at penetrating through the defensive lines to find his teammates, giving Arsenal another option to move the ball forward with.

The development of Rice is key in securing a new contract at Arsenal.

The 27-year-old has already proven to be one of the most reliable members of Arteta’s squad and has shown great longevity by only missing five games out of his 119 Premier League outings in his first spell at the club.

Former West Ham teammate Jack Wilshere has also praised Rice’s strong work ethic, ability to adapt and natural leadership qualities.

The reported 5-year deal will keep Rice at Arsenal until 2031, into his early 30s. While the terms of the deal have yet to be signed, it is a huge statement from the club.

They are not only rewarding Rice for what he has done but also investing in what he can do.

With Arteta finding new ways to utilise Rice’s qualities in midfield, Rice could still have some of his best years ahead of him.

Today, Massimiliano Mirabelli has looked back on his time at AC Milan and his favourite signing from his short time with the club.

After arriving before the 2017 summer transfer window, there was hope that Mirabelli would oversee a new era with Milan. However, he never got given the extended period to oversee that change that he’d hoped for.

Indeed, just a year later, the Elliott fund arrived and he was removed from his role as the Rossoneri truly entered a new era.

Responsible for the signings of Andre Silva, Andrea Conti and most notably Leonardo Bonucci, you wonder what would’ve happened if he’d stayed with the Rossoneri for longer. Nevertheless, with what and who eventually arrived, there’s no point considering the alternative, especially given he took a four-year break after his time with the club.

Speaking to Tuttosport, via MilanNews, Mirabelli spoke about the disappointment of his time with the Rossoneri and his pride in bringing Hakan Calhanoglu to Italy.

“Unfortunately, the experience in Milan was short-lived due to the change of ownership. It left a bitter taste in our mouths because we weren’t given the opportunity to complete a long-term project.

“Roma wasn’t built in a day… The deal that still makes me proud today? The Calhanoglu deal . Without a doubt, the best and most successful gamble of my career. We’re talking about the man who, for me, remains the most complete midfielder in Europe to this day. He has incredible, uncommon qualities.

“I started following him when I worked at Inter. We couldn’t sign him from Leverkusen due to an ongoing lawsuit over his double registration as a minor, which resulted in his suspension.

“That’s why I call it a “gamble”: I wanted him at Milan at all costs, even though he hadn’t played for a year and had essentially disappeared from the radar. I was heavily criticized. But, I repeat, I was convinced I had a boy with enormous potential on my hands.

“The biggest discussion, then, was about his position. We spent hours with Montella and—later—with Gattuso to understand where he could best express himself. At Leverkusen, he’d played everywhere: as a midfielder, as an attacking midfielder, and even as a winger.

“Initially, we played him on the left flank, with an inverted foot, to allow him to cut inside and shoot. But it was clear to me that he had all the makings of a great playmaker. When I suggested it to the right people, they called me crazy…

“Leaving for zero? Don’t make me think about it… It’s unspeakable, unbelievable.”

#2187. Martin Zubimendi sends fresh Arsenal message as January transfer speculation grows

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Martin Zubimendi has raised questions among Arsenal fans over his future. This follows comments from the midfielder that he is keen to succeed in north London despite struggling for game time this season.

The Spain international has been linked with a move to Chelsea, Atletico Madrid and Real Madrid ahead of the January transfer window. Reports from Spain have said that Zubimendi’s situation at the club is becoming more complex, with the player possibly being available.

Zubimendi has also spoken about the speculation. In his interview during the international break with Marca, he said he did not feel like he was near to a move to Atletico, and he is still delighted to be in London.

He added the 27-year-old has had no contact with any other clubs. His priority is to get into Arteta’s squad.

This is an interesting response to the transfer speculation that has been building. Arsenal may well attract interest for one of their midfielders. However, there does not appear to be any evidence that Zubimendi is actively looking to leave.

In his first season, he played 38 times in the Premier League and became an important member of Arteta’s midfield. However, he has not started a league game this season.

Opta Analyst shows that he has only played 100 minutes in the Premier League in seven games. He has also faced increased competition for a spot in the team with the arrival of Bruno Guimarães.

Myles Lewis-Skelly is also an option in the middle of the park. Zubimendi will have to fight for his place back.

It is understood Arteta had a chat with Zubimendi ahead of the campaign and made it clear he would face more competition from the new signings and would have to earn his place.

Zubimendi has accepted the challenge and has said he could see it as a chance to learn and improve due to his reduced workload.

The interest from Spain and Chelsea will make it a tough choice if the right offers come in. However, losing Zubimendi will mean Arteta won’t have as many options to call upon in the busy second half of the season.

But he proved his worth in the game against Slavia Prague earlier this month. He started the Champions League match and played the full 90 minutes.

This shows that his contribution shouldn’t be judged purely on how much he plays in the Premier League. Arsenal will have a busy schedule in Europe and domestically, and he may provide some much-needed quality in the midfield.

With Zubimendi contracted until 2030, there is no immediate reason for Arsenal to look to sell him. He will have time to fight for his spot in the starting XI.

Despite not making a commitment to remain at the club beyond January, his remarks indicate that he is not on the lookout for alternatives.

The priority for Arsenal will be trying to find a way to get the best out of Zubimendi. He was a regular last season and seems happy to wait for his chance.

#2186. Chelsea sweat on key stars as Bournemouth test looms

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Chelsea return to Premier League action this weekend with a growing treatment table and a lot of questions for Xabi Alonso to answer before Saturday’s visit of Bournemouth.

The main concern revolves around Joao Pedro. Chelsea’s current player of the season rescued a point against newly promoted Hull, but he suffered a knock and sat out the damaging 3-0 defeat at Brentford. The problem also prompted his removal from Brazil’s squad shortly after he had returned to it.

It is understood that he is undergoing specialist knee treatment in an attempt to expedite his recovery, although reports in Brazil suggest that he could be out for up to four weeks. No date has yet been set, but a return later in October looks most likely.

There is more good news elsewhere. Cole Palmer was among the players called up to the latest England squad, which did not go down too well with Thomas Tuchel.

The midfielder has been recovering from a persistent groin injury and has been working with a rehab coach. He is likely to be fit this weekend.

Moises Caicedo has also struggled with an injury this season, playing just 14 minutes across the campaign. He’s spent the majority of time on the bench recovering from a bad calf injury, which ruled him out for most of the season.

He made his only appearance as a substitute against his former club Brighton, but the game was red-flagged for Ecuador’s friendlies, so he did not play.

Alonso said before the Brentford match that the player would be ready to return. It seems to be the case, unless something went wrong.

Captain Reece James missed the Brentford game with yet another hamstring problem. Alonso said he only just missed out, so a return seems likely.

There may also be debuts. Marco Palestra, the £27m signing from Atalanta, has been sidelined since a training injury just before the opening match against Fulham. He is back in full training and was expected to feature in a behind-closed-doors friendly with Portsmouth. Chelsea lost 2-0. Emmanuel Emegha, injured after scoring against Bromley in July, is also pushing for involvement.

Less clear is the position of Jamie Gittens. He limped off with an ankle injury playing for England Under-21s against Kazakhstan and was seen on crutches. Mike Penders is fine after a quad scare on Belgium duty. Returning to the bench against Turkey.

Chelsea badly need a lift. Defeat at Brentford before the break left Alonso’s side looking shaky, and a squad stretched by injuries has not helped.

Saturday brings a chance to steady things. If Palmer, James and Caicedo are available, with Palestra and Emegha potentially adding fresh options, the picture will look much brighter at Stamford Bridge. Joao Pedro’s absence will still sting. However, a strong response against Bournemouth would go a long way towards easing the pressure and restoring belief.

#2185. Chelsea co-sporting director Laurence Stewart to leave in leadership reshuffle

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club opting to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

The departure is understood to be amicable and won’t take immediate effect. Stewart could continue attending matches before formally leaving, following discussions with co-owner Behdad Eghbali over the club’s evolving sporting structure.

The story was first reported by Matt Law of The Telegraph and Nizaar Kinsella of BBC Sport, with Ben Jacobs subsequently providing further details about how Chelsea intend to operate.

Stewart arrived from Monaco in October 2022 as technical director before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley in February 2023.

Chelsea’s day-to-day sporting leadership will now be shared between Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields, each with defined responsibilities.

Winstanley will continue overseeing transfer negotiations, player contracts and several departments at Cobham, including medical and performance operations. Shields’ work centres on the academy and player pathways, while Jewell has wider responsibilities across the BlueCo operation, including Strasbourg.

According to Jacobs, discussions about restructuring began in April and accelerated following the appointment of Xabi Alonso as head coach.

Stewart had overseen investment in Chelsea’s data analysis operation, although Jewell and Shields have increasingly taken responsibility for using that information in recruitment and talent identification.

Dave Fallows will remain involved in an advisory capacity, rather than taking on a fixed day-to-day portfolio.

The changes are being described as a natural evolution of Chelsea’s sporting model. There is no suggestion that Stewart’s departure followed a breakdown in relations, and the club are not currently planning to recruit a replacement.

There will be no live UK television coverage of Chelsea’s return to Premier League action against Bournemouth on Saturday afternoon.

Xabi Alonso’s side host the Cherries at Stamford Bridge in a traditional 3pm kick-off, with the fixture falling within the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

The importance of retaining Raya and Timber is evident.

Arteta recently signed a contract extension until 2030. They aren’t just trying to keep their best players for another year but are looking to create consistency within the squad and management.

This is an issue for any club competing within the domestic and European leagues, as it can cause problems with team cohesion and development due to constant change of key players.

While there is still work to be done, this is positive news for fans.

Signing Raya and Timber will ensure that two key components of Arteta’s backline are safe. It will also show that with Rice’s new contract, they’re looking to build on their recent success and not let their best players near the end of their contracts.

#2178. Arsenal mourns Peter Storey: 1971 Double-winning hero dies aged 81

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have expressed their condolences in the passing of former player Peter Storey.

Storey was an influential member of one of the most successful sides in the club’s history. The midfielder appeared in 501 games for the Gunners between 1965 and 1977, winning the First Division and FA Cup during the 1970/71 season.

Storey was a key figure in the famous run as he helped keep the FA Cup hopes alive with a display against Stoke City.

A statement from the club confirmed his death on Tuesday (17th November). The club will pay tribute to Storey in their Champions League tie against Borussia Dortmund on 24th November.

Storey signed as an apprentice with Arsenal in 1961, and made his first-team debut in October 1965 in a game against Leicester City.

He was later converted to a central midfielder by manager Bertie Mee and coach Don Howe. Storey became an invaluable member of the squad due to his aggressive play, work-rate and versatility.

In 1970 Storey was part of the side that won the Inter-Cities Fairs Cup, and the following year Arsenal won the league and FA Cup double.

Storey played a key role in an amazing FA Cup semi-final in April 1971. After Arsenal had looked like they were losing, Storey scored a last-minute penalty to make it 2-2.

Arsenal won the replay to get to the final, beating Liverpool to win their first-ever double.

Storey scored 17 goals in 501 games for Arsenal. This puts him in joint 8th place in the most appearances for the club.

Storey enjoyed success at club level and earned his first cap for England in 1971. He earned a total of 19 caps for England.

He played for England until 1973. In 1977, Storey left Arsenal to join Fulham, meaning he had a 12-year stint at the north London club.

He played 19 games for Fulham before retiring from professional football in 1978.

Following his football career, Storey had a difficult time. He was imprisoned for a number of crimes. In 2010, he wrote an autobiography, True Storey: My Life and Crimes as a Football Hatchet Man, detailing these events in his life.

He will be remembered in Arsenal history for his impressive contribution to the club. Having made 501 appearances for the club, he was a key member of the squad that won the Double in 1971.

Playing against Borussia Dortmund will be a great way for fans to pay tribute to one of the key players in a special time in the history of the club.

He has returned to training so could make the starting XI after coming on as a substitute in the game against Fulham. Since moving to the club from France, he has played 6 games in the Premier League.

Despite Joelinton being absent, Newcastle will still be a dangerous opponent. A calm performance from Arsenal will be required in order to take 3 points from the match.

With Joelinton missing, Arsenal can assert control in midfield. As they look to stay on top of the league table, exploiting the injury issues in Newcastle’s team should be a priority.

#2175. Christos Tzolis challenges Arsenal critics with a message about his physicality

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Winger Christos Tzolis wants to prove he has a lot to offer Arsenal beyond his attacking prowess.

Since moving to the club from Club Brugge in July, the 24-year-old has made a great start to life at the club after an impressive season in Belgium. The Greek attacker scored 17 goals and made 23 assists in 36 league games.

But doubts were raised about his ease of integration into the Premier League. In his first stint in England with Norwich City, he showed little sign of becoming the player he is now.

Since making his debut for the club, Tzolis has become a more rounded attacking threat. He has started all 7 Premier League games this season and provides a reliable option on the left for Arteta.

However, in recent comments, it seems that he is aware of the perceptions around his physicality and is keen to change them.

Tzolis spoke to Arsenal Media, saying that he doesn’t think the notion of him lacking the physicality to play at top level has validity.

He claimed that he is not a player without muscle and cited his accomplishments. He also described his tendency to use aggression against his opponents.

This is a key part of Arteta’s system at the club. Tzolis is expected to press from the flanks if Arsenal lose the ball.

Tzolis is comfortable with this role. He is able to track back well to support his full-back, adding an extra dimension to the left side.

The Greek international has proven his physical ability outside of match play. He was nicknamed Leonidas by the fitness team at the club due to a strong performance in pre-season.

It is reported that he completed the running challenge better than anyone in the squad.

Tzolis has joined Arsenal to add attacking flair to the squad, having left for Besiktas. He looks like he could be a formidable goalscorer and creator.

In the league last season, Tzolis contributed 40 goals and put in a good performance in Europe. This led to Arsenal spending a reported £34 million to bring him to the club.

He has not had the best impact in the league, registering just one assist and no goals, but this should not be taken to mean he is not contributing.

Tzolis has shown an ability to fit into Arteta’s style of play. His defensive contribution, work rate and combative nature may prove very valuable in tough away games.

Despite there being plenty to improve on, Tzolis provides more than just an attacking option for Arsenal.

If Tzolis can change people’s view of him in terms of his physicality and continues to put his technical skills and determination together, then he can be a valuable asset in Arteta’s plans.

#2173. Arsenal transfer dilemma: Alex Scott and Nico Paz could force difficult decisions for Arteta

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Potential signings of Alex Scott and Nico Paz may pose a challenge for Arteta.

While both players would add quality to the squad, their arrival would be a consideration for current players in the Emirates squad.

Scott would add quality in midfield, and Paz adds creativity and versatility going forward. Arteta must consider that adding these players may hinder the development of current players.

Arsenal are believed to be keen on bringing Alex Scott to the club.

After rejecting contract offers from Bournemouth, Scott could only have one year left on his contract at the club next summer. However, he is understood to be valued by Bournemouth at over £100m.

He would be a great addition for Arteta, with the versatility and skill he has. He could give added competition in midfield.

Yet his potential arrival raises questions about Myles Lewis-Skelly’s future role.

Myles Lewis-Skelly was included in the Champions League final line-up last season. He showed that he is trusted by the club.He has also demonstrated his commitment by remaining at the club despite opportunities to gain regular playing time at other clubs.

Signing another expensive midfielder may present difficulties. It is unclear at this point whether Scott will be a useful addition to the squad or simply overcrowd this area of the team.

Nico Paz presents another interesting option for Arsenal.

Paz joined Como from Real Madrid in the summer. He has caught the eye of a number of big clubs and has increased his appeal with strong performances in international football. The 22-year-old has scored 5 times in his last 5 games for Argentina.

Paz is a versatile right-sided player and could be an appealing addition to Arteta’s strike force.

There are already plenty of players fighting for attacking positions at Arsenal.

Bukayo Saka is key in the team’s attack, and with Martin Ødegaard and Noni Madueke in the squad too, there are plenty of options. Max Dowman is a young player looking to break into the first team.

While Paz would add quality to the squad, Arsenal would need to find a specific role for him. There are complications with the move, though. Real Madrid have an option to re-sign him in 2027 for 80 million euros.

Although Arsenal are facing challenges in attracting quality players to the club. They now also face issues in managing a squad with experienced internationals and developing young players.

Although Scott and Paz would improve the depth of the squad, players must not be sacrificed for new players if they have already shown quality.

Players such as Lewis-Skelly and Dowman may be negatively affected by this. Opportunities may be taken away from them if the club continues to spend large amounts of money on new players.

However, it is still very important for Arsenal to bring in quality players to compete for major titles.

Know how the additions will integrate within the playing style and how they affect the other players within the squad.

The Gunners can strengthen further without overloading the squad. Good additions can be made to the first XI whilst also providing a realistic chance for young players to come through from within the academy.

#2172. Max Dowman’s Arsenal return: Is the teenage star one to watch, and can he keep his nose clean?

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Max Dowman shortly returns to action for Arsenal after serving a three-game suspension.

His absence has been disappointing, but the experience should give the youngster an important lesson. Can he put the incident behind him and show Mikel Arteta that he can be trusted?

Dowman received a red card against Fleetwood Town after kicking out at Harrison Neal. Arsenal appealed the decision, but the FA rejected the club’s appeal.

The suspension ruled him out of three domestic fixtures against Liverpool, Hull City and Newcastle United.

He’ll be back on 29 November as Arsenal take on Manchester City. This should give the young star a chance to get his season back on track and showcase what makes him such a highly-rated player.

Dowman looks very promising for the future. He has shown great adaptability playing with the more experienced players and has the self-belief to make an impact in games.

He has performed well in the Carabao Cup. In the 4-2 win against Ipswich, Dowman scored two goals, showing his flair and poise in front of goal.

Arteta has given him opportunities to play in the Premier League and Champions League, meaning that he appears to have a clear route into the first team.

Although Dowman still has a lot to learn, he displays great potential in his technical ability and combative nature. As he gets more games under his belt, fans will be excited to see how he progresses.

Dowman was sent off in an unfortunate moment. He was heavily challenged by Neal and, after lashing out, was shown a straight red card.

Although this one moment should not be enough to dismiss a young player from the game completely, it can serve as a valuable lesson on the pressure that comes with playing at a higher level.

Opponents will test Dowman physically, but he must learn to keep his composure. As he starts making an impact, some players may target his age and inexperience to unsettle him.

As he gets more game time, consistency will be key. Arteta must be confident that Dowman can be relied on in high pressure situations.

Arsenal do not need to rush Dowman into the first team. The squad’s quality allows Arteta to manage his minutes and give him time to develop without putting too much pressure on him.

If Dowman is to play against Manchester City, it will depend on Arteta’s plans, but he has certainly done enough to warrant consideration.

The suspension should not define his season. What matters now is how he responds, both on and off the pitch.

Dowman has the talent to become an important player for Arsenal. Keeping his discipline will be just as important as displaying his ability.

#2170. Arsenal transfer news: Why Kevin Schade could be a smart January signing

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal are looking to strengthen their attacking options in January. One player they are reportedly keeping an eye on is Brentford winger Kevin Schade. Mikel Arteta will be keen to add competition in attack, and Schade may be a good option.

Schade will give additional pace to the offence. He is capable of playing in multiple attacking positions to provide Arteta with extra depth in the squad.

However, it may be difficult to get Schade. He is on loan at Brentford, and may want to keep him in the long term. If Schade is seen as a key player in the future, they may be difficult to negotiate with.

Schade’s versatility is another big plus. His speed and straightforward method make him hard to deal with for defenders. In addition, he can be used as a left-sided attacker, giving Arteta more flexibility in his front line. As the club are playing in a number of competitions, this can be very useful.

Schade has already scored 3 times in the Premier League this season. He has quickly adapted to playing in England after moving from Freiburg to Brentford in 2023.

Despite only being 24 years old, Schade still has a lot to improve on. He has lots of experience in the Premier League but has not quite reached the peak of his ability yet. If he moves to Arsenal, he may be able to elevate his performance.

Brentford has already started negotiations with Schade regarding a contract extension that would keep him tied to the club for a long time. He is already contracted until the summer of 2028.

Arsenal’s interest could prove problematic. Other clubs are also keeping their eyes on this player. Tottenham in particular are not seen as a major threat at this stage.

It is going to be very challenging for Brentford to let go of Schade because he has played a key part in their offensive setup, and losing Schade could prove disastrous.

Arsenal will have to pay Brentford whatever they ask for, Schade, and since he has a decent amount of time left on his contract, Brentford has an edge in negotiations.

Arsenal’s interest in Schade is logical. His versatility will provide Arteta with additional flexibility because of his prior experience in the Premier League.

It can be considered positive to sign new strikers. Arsenal must decide if they believe that Schade will fit in well with their current players and whether the asking price from Brentford is reasonable.

As of now, there are no signs of a transfer on the horizon, but it is clear that Schade is a player worth monitoring as the winter transfer window approaches.

The Mag Podcast Episode 18: Newcastle United 2020-21 featuring Steve Bruce, Joe Willock and the Road to takeover

Published: 18/11/2026 | Duration: 55 mins | Hosts: Jamie Smith, Simon Cahill & David Hutchinson

In Episode 18 of The Mag Pod, we find Jamie, Simon, and Hutch reflecting back on the time of Covid and a pre-Newcastle United takeover era that featured Steve Bruce, Mike Ashley and Joe Willock. A strange time indeed to be a United fan.

You can play episode 18 on the audio player above or use your favourite podcast platforms including Spotify, Apple Podcasts, Amazon Music, YouTube, and Acast.

Listen to the latest episode of The Mag Pod! Join Jamie, Simon, and Hutch for unfiltered football debate.

Newcastle United’s difficult 2020-21 season under Steve Bruce, Joe Willock’s remarkable loan spell and the build-up to the Saudi-backed takeover are the focus as Jamie Smith, Simon Cahill and David Hutchinson return for the final instalment of their international break specials.

The trio revisit football behind closed doors, compare the pre-takeover squad with today’s Newcastle United and look ahead to the return of Bruno Guimarães with Arsenal.

The 2020-21 campaign was a strange time to be a Newcastle supporter. With St James’ Park largely empty due to Covid restrictions, Mike Ashley still in charge and Steve Bruce overseeing some desperately uninspiring football, the connection between supporters and their club was arguably at its lowest point.

Simon takes us back through the season, from the arrival of Callum Wilson and Ryan Fraser to the struggles of a side relying heavily on Allan Saint-Maximin for attacking inspiration. Despite spending much of the campaign uncomfortably close to the relegation places, Newcastle eventually finished 12th, thanks in no small part to Joe Willock’s extraordinary impact after joining on loan from Arsenal.

There’s also a moving personal story from Simon about his family’s experiences during lockdown, providing a reminder that football was only one part of an extraordinary period in everyone’s lives.

The discussion then turns to the present.

Has Newcastle’s squad improved as much as it should have since the takeover? Are there players from Bruce’s team who would strengthen today’s starting eleven? And how much patience should supporters have with the club’s latest recruits?

With the international break finally ending, there’s a round up of Newcastle players representing their countries before attention switches to Arsenal’s visit and the potentially awkward reception awaiting former Magpies favourite Bruno Guimarães.

Plenty of nostalgia, disagreement and the usual irreverent humour in another edition of The Mag Pod.

#2168. Ramos speaks out after disappointing Portugal result vs. Denmark: “Something that happens”

AC Milan are, once again, left looking at a disappointing performance from Goncalo Ramos; this time it was for Portugal.

It’s fair to say that Ramos’ start to life with Milan has been unsatisfactory. The Portuguese started brightly in his first two games, but has since struggled to really hit the highs expected of him after an almost €90 million deal was agreed in the summer.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

With another international break, the hope was that he’d rediscover the form from the last set of games and bring it back to San Siro, but it hasn’t happened that way yet.

Speaking in the mixed zone after Portugal’s heavy defeat, Ramos looked at what went wrong in the game and what needs to be corrected next time, as MilanPress relays.

“I think it was a tough match. They studied our team well, and even the details ultimately fell in their favour. Then we ended up losing momentum a bit, but that’s something that happens and is part of it.

“We’re first in the most important group. We already have another match in three days, so it’s not worth dwelling too much on what happened today. There’s already a match ahead, and it’s very important.”

“In the end, they managed to apply better pressure on us. We weren’t able to find many options, and that ended up hurting our game because we like to have clean, controlled possession of the ball, and that’s what makes the difference in our game.

“On the one hand, it’s good to have a match in three days and not have to spend three or four months thinking about a defeat like that. We already have a game to respond and to erase what happened here today.

“The conversation in the locker room after the game was that we’re first and we have a game to clean up after what happened today. That’s the goal of the team and the group.”

Considering he’s now the out-and-out first-choice striker for Portugal, it’s a lot of pressure on his shoulders for club and country. Hopefully he can start to thrive under the pressure, rather than succumbing to it.
